93
) and
\mathcal{B}=F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
as (
95
). We use the resolvent identity and the fact that
\left(J^{(r)}_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}Q_{n}=Q_{n-2mn^{\beta}}\left(J^{(r)}_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}Q_{n}
to obtain

\left(\left(J^{(r)}_{n+2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}-\left(J^{(r)}_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}\right)Q_{n}=-\left(J^{(r)}_{n+2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}\tilde{E}\left(J^{(r)}_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}Q_{n},

where
\tilde{E}
only has two entries that are not zeros, i.e.,

\begin{cases}(\tilde{E})_{i,j}=(\tilde{E})_{j,i}=a_{n-2mn^{\beta}},&\quad\text{for $i=n-2mn^{\beta}$ and $j=n-2mn^{\beta}-1$,}\\
(\tilde{E})_{i,j}=0,&\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(106)

Note that the entries of matrix
\tilde{E}\left(J^{(r)}_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}Q_{n}
are zeros except for those at the last
2mn^{\beta}
columns of the first row. Hence,

\left|\mathcal{C}_{2}^{(n)}(F_{n+2mn^{\beta}})-\mathcal{C}_{2}^{(n)}(F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}})\right|\\
\leq\sum_{r=1}^{2M}|c_{r}|\left\|\left(J^{(r)}_{n+2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\tilde{E}\left(J^{(r)}_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\right)^{-1}Q_{n}\right\|_{1}\bigg(\|F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}\|_{\infty}+\|F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\|_{\infty}\bigg).

(107)

Similarly, by (
97
) of Proposition
4.1
, we have that (
107
) is exponentially small.

The estimate (
107
) show that
\left|\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(F_{n+2mn^{\beta}})-\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}})\right|
is exponentially small for
m=2
.

For the general case
m>2
, the algebra of the difference of the cumulants is more complicated than (
104
). However, the strategy is similar. For details, see the complete proof of Proposition
4.2
in Section
5.4
.
∎

Proposition
4.2
implies that it is sufficient to study the truncated operator
F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
as
n\to\infty
. By the definition of
F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
in (
95
), essentially, we are left to study the resolvent
\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}
defined in (
94
).

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
\beta=\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{3}
such that
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}<1
. Assume conditions in Theorem
2.3
are satisfied. Consider
x_{0}
to be near the edges, i.e., (
24
). Then there exists a decomposition of
\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}
such that

\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}=T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})+H_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})

(108)

where
T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})
and
H_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})
are such that as
n\to\infty
, for all
j,k=n-2mn^{\beta},\dots,n+2mn^{\beta}
,

(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r}))_{j,k}=\frac{s(-s)^{|k-j|}\prod_{l=\min\{j,k\}}^{\max\{j,k\}-1}\left(1-\left(\frac{-s\eta_{r}}{a_{n,n}n^{\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\xi_{l}^{(r)}\right)}{2a_{n,n}\left(\frac{-s\eta_{r}}{a_{n,n}n^{\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(1+o(1)\right),

(109)

where
s=1
for left edges and
s=-1
for right edges and
\max_{l=n-2mn^{\beta},\dots,n+2mn^{\beta}}|\xi_{l}^{(r)}|=o(n^{-\alpha/2})
, and by convention
\prod_{l=j}^{j-1}\equiv 1
. We also have as
n\to\infty
, for all
j,k,j+l,k+l=n-2mn^{\beta},\dots,n+2mn^{\beta}
,

\frac{(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta))_{j,k}}{(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta))_{j+l,k+l}}=1+o\left(n^{-\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right),

(110)

\left|(H_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r}))_{j,k}\right|\leq Cn^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\left(e^{-dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\max\{j,k\}-n+2mn^{\beta})}+e^{-dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(n+2mn^{\beta}-\min\{j,k\})}\right),

(111)

for some constant
C,d>0
.

For the proof of Proposition
4.3
, see Section
6.2
.

Compared with (
77
), (
108
) can be regarded as an analogue of the inverse formula of a Toeplitz operator. Formula (
109
) implies that the entries along the same diagonals of
T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
are almost the same. The error is controlled by (
110
).

Note that the trace norm of
H_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})
is much smaller than that of the
T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})
by (
111
). We will also show that its contribution to the cumulant is exponentially small in the following proposition.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\beta=\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{3}
such that
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}<1
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
m\geq 2
. Assume conditions in Theorem
2.3
are satisfied. Consider
x_{0}
to be at the edges. Then there exists a constant
C_{m}^{\prime}>0
that only depends on
m
and
M
such that

\left|n^{\alpha m}\mathcal{C}_{m}(X^{(n)}_{f,\alpha,x_{0}})-\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}\left(\sum_{r=1}^{2M}c_{r}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)\right)\right|\leq C_{m}^{\prime}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(112)

for all
n>n_{0}
for some constant
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
.

For the proof of Proposition
4.4
, see Section
6.3
.

Proposition
4.4
reduces asymptotic analysis of the cumulants to the matrices
T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})
. These matrices have exponential off-diagonal decay and approximately constant diagonals, but are not exactly Toeplitz. We will, in Section
7
, give a quantitative estimate of the cumulant generating function of
\sum_{r}c_{r}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)
, which is controlled by the trace of a truncated commutator. This results in the following proposition.

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
\beta=\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{3}
such that
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}<1
. Assume conditions in Theorem
2.3
are satisfied. Consider
x_{0}
to be at the edges. The test function
f
is defined as (
43
). Then

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{(n)}\left(\sum_{r=1}^{2M}c_{r}n^{-\alpha}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)\right)=\begin{cases}\sigma_{f}^{2},\quad&k=2\\
0,\quad&k=3,\dots m.\end{cases}

(113)

where,
\sigma_{f}^{2}
is given by (
26
) and (
27
).

Consequently, Theorem
2.3
holds for such test functions
f
as defined as (
43
).

For the proof of Proposition
4.5
, see Section
7.2
.

The last step of the proof of Theorem
2.3
is to extend such
f
to compactly supported functions following a standard procedure, for example,
[
7
,
17
]
. This is elaborated in Section
4.2
.

4.2
Proof of Theorem
2.3

To finish the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we need to extend the result of Proposition
4.5
to the test functions
f\in C^{1}_{c}
. An important role of extension is played by the following space of functions.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{w}
be a space of functions of
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\lim_{x\to\pm\infty}f(x)=0
and

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}\coloneqq\sup_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{1+x^{2}}\sqrt{1+y^{2}}\left|\frac{f(x)-f(y)}{x-y}\right|<\infty.

(114)

\mathcal{L}_{w}
is a normed space with the weighted Lipschitz norm
\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}
. Note that
C_{c}^{1}\subset\mathcal{L}_{w}
.

Proposition 4.6

(Proposition 5.1 in
[
7
]
)
.

Let
g(x)=\frac{1}{x-i}
. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{L}_{w}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}^{2}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)+\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)\right).

(115)

A consequence of Proposition
4.6
is the following corollary.

Corollary 4.1
.

Assume all conditions of Theorem
2.3
are satisfied. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{L}_{w}
we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)\leq\frac{1}{8}\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}^{2}.

(116)

Proof.

We are to estimate the right-hand side of (
115
). Note that for
g(x)=\frac{1}{x-i}
we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g(x)=\frac{1}{2i}\left(\frac{1}{x-i}-\frac{1}{x+i}\right),\qquad\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g(x)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{x-i}+\frac{1}{x+i}\right).

Using Proposition
4.5
for these two functions, we find that at the right edge

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g(-x^{2})-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g(-y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy=\frac{3}{32},

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g(-x^{2})-\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g(-y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy=\frac{1}{32}.

Similarly, we also obtain that at the left edge

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g(x^{2})-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g(y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy=\frac{3}{32},

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g(x^{2})-\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g(y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy=\frac{1}{32}.

These imply that at both edges

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}g,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)+\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}g,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)\right)\leq\frac{1}{8}.

Then combine with Proposition
4.6
and we conclude this corollary. ∎

Lemma 4.1

(Lemma 5.3 in
[
7
]
)
.

Let
f\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an
M\in\mathbb{N}
,
d_{r}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\lambda_{r}\in\mathbb{C}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\lambda_{r})>0
for all
r=1,\dots,M
such that,

\left\|f(x)-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\sum_{r=1}^{M}\frac{d_{r}}{x-\lambda_{r}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}<\varepsilon,

(117)

where the weighted Lipschitz norm is defined in Definition
4.1
.

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
2.3
.

Proof of Theorem
2.3
.

Recall that we define
f_{\alpha,x_{0}}(x)\coloneqq f(n^{\alpha}(x-x_{0}))
. By the inequality
|1-e^{ix}|\leq|x|
for all real
x
, we have
|e^{ix}-e^{iy}|\leq|x-y|
for any
x,y
real. Then use Jensen’s inequality to deduce the following bound, for any real-valued functions
f,h
and real number
t
,

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}-\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}\right|\leq t\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\left(X_{f-h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right)}.

(118)

Moreover, define

\sigma_{f,L}^{2}\coloneqq\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(x^{2})-f(y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy,\quad\sigma_{f,R}^{2}\coloneqq\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(-x^{2})-f(-y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy.

(119)

We have for both
j\in\{L,R\}
, by Definition
4.1
,

\sigma_{f,j}^{2}\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}^{2}\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{x+y}{\sqrt{x^{4}+1}\sqrt{y^{4}+1}}\right)^{2}dxdy.

(120)

Use Cauchy residue theorem to compute the double integral
\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{x+y}{\sqrt{x^{4}+1}\sqrt{y^{4}+1}}\right)^{2}dxdy=\pi^{2}
. Hence,

\sigma_{f,j}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}^{2}.

(121)

Moreover, use the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality to obtain for both
j\in\{L,R\}

\left|\sigma_{f,j}^{2}-\sigma_{h,j}\right|\leq\sqrt{\sigma_{f+h,j}^{2}\sigma_{f-h,j}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{8}\|f-h\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}\|f+h\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}.

(122)

Then use triangle inequality, (
118
), (
121
), (
122
) and Corollary
4.1
, to obtain the following, as
n\to\infty
,

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sigma_{f,j}^{2}}\right|\\
\leq\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}-\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}\right|+\left|e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sigma_{f,j}^{2}}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sigma_{h,j}^{2}}\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sigma_{h,j}^{2}}\right|\\
\leq t\left(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\|f-h\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}+o(1)\right)+\frac{t^{2}}{16}\|f-h\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}\|f+h\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sigma_{h,j}^{2}}\right|.

(123)

Let
f\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and any
\varepsilon>0
. By Lemma
4.1
, there exists

h=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\sum_{r=1}^{M}\frac{d_{r}}{x-\lambda_{r}}

such that

\left\|f-h\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}<\varepsilon.

(124)

Moreover, since
f\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, we have
\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}<\infty
. By triangle inequality, we have

\left\|f+h\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}<2\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}+\varepsilon<\infty.

Apply Proposition
4.5
to
h
and we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{h,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sigma_{h,j}^{2}}\right|=0.

(125)

Plug (
124
) and (
125
) into (
123
) and we get, for any
j=L
or
R
, and any
\varepsilon>0
,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sigma_{f,j}^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{t\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{2}}+\frac{t^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{16}\left(2\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{w}}+\varepsilon\right).

Hence

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{itX_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}}\right]e^{-it\mathbb{E}\left[X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\right]}-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sigma_{f,j}^{2}}\right|=0.

This completes the proof of Theorem
2.3
for any
f\in C^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
at both left and right edges.
∎

Note that the proof of Propositions
4.1
,
4.2
,
4.3
and
4.4
is postponed to Sections
5
and
6
below.

5
Proof of Propositions
4.1
and
4.2

In this section we are to prove Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
.

In Subsection
5.1
, we continue the discussion in Subsection
3.5
and derive Proposition
5.1
, a general theory of tri-diagonal matrices that will be used to prove Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
.

In Subsection
5.2
, we estimate the resolvent of both
J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
and the middle block of
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
defined in (
93
) and (
94
) by Proposition
5.1
. Then, complete the proof of Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
.

5.1
Inverse of a Tri-diagonal Matrix: Part I

The goal of this subsection is to estimate the inverse of the tri-diagonal symmetric matrix
J_{N}
defined in (
79
) entry-wise.

Proposition 5.1
.

Consider an
N\times N
non singular tri-diagonal symmetric matrix
J_{N}
defined as (
79
) with entries
a_{j},b_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
,
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
and
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z\neq 0
. Let
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
with
N_{1}<N
. Assume
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(b_{j}-z)>0
for all
j\geq N_{0}
.

Let
\omega_{j}^{+},\omega_{j}^{-}
be defined as (
87
) and
M_{j}
be defined as (
88
). Denote

c=\left|\frac{-\omega_{N-1}^{+}a_{N-1}+b_{N-1}-z}{\omega_{N-1}^{-}a_{N-1}-b_{N-1}+z}\right|,

(126)

\varepsilon_{1}=(N-N_{1})\max_{l\geq N_{1}}\|M_{l}\|_{\infty}.

(127)

and

\varepsilon_{2}=\prod_{l=N_{1}}^{N-1}\left|\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}\right|.

(128)

Assume that
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
and
4(1+c)\varepsilon_{1}+c\varepsilon_{2}<1
.
Then for all
j\leq N_{1}<k
we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(J_{N}^{-1}\right)_{j,k}\right|\leq\frac{1+4(1+c)\varepsilon_{1}+c}{1-4(1+c)\varepsilon_{1}-c\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|J_{N}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\prod_{l=N_{1}}^{k-1}\left|\omega_{l}^{+}\right|^{-1}.

(129)

Proof.

Apply the inverse formula for tri-diagonal matrix (
86
) to obtain, for
j\leq N_{1}<k
,

\left(J_{N}^{-1}\right)_{j,k}=(-1)^{k-N_{1}}\frac{\beta_{k}}{\beta_{N_{1}}}\left(J_{N}^{-1}\right)_{j,N_{1}}.

(130)

Recall that
\beta_{j}
is defined recursively by (
85
), i.e.,

\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{k}\\
\beta_{k-1}\end{pmatrix}=A_{k}A_{k+1}\cdots A_{N-1}\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{N}\\
\beta_{N-1}\end{pmatrix},\quad\beta_{N}=a_{N-1},\quad\beta_{N-1}=b_{N-1}-z,

(131)

where the matrix
A_{k}
is defined as (
84
). Recall that the eigenvalues
\omega_{k}^{+}
and
\omega_{k}^{-}
of
A_{k}
are defined as (
87
). Then
A_{k}
can be diagonalised as
A_{k}=V_{k}\Omega_{k}V_{k}^{-1}
where
V_{k}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
\omega_{k}^{+}&\omega_{k}^{-}\end{pmatrix}
and
\Omega_{k}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\omega_{k}^{+}&0\\
0&\omega_{k}^{-}\end{pmatrix}
.
Further define
C_{N}\coloneqq 0
and

C_{k}\coloneqq V_{k}^{-1}\left(A_{k}A_{k+1}\dots A_{N-1}-V_{k}\left(\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\Omega_{l}\right)V_{N-1}^{-1}\right)V_{N-1},\quad k=1,2,\dots,N-1

(132)

Rewrite (
131
) for
k=1,2,\dots,N-1
as

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{k}\\
\beta_{k-1}\end{pmatrix}=

\displaystyle V_{k}\left(C_{k}+\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\Omega_{l}\right)V_{N-1}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{N}\\
\beta_{N-1}\end{pmatrix}.

(133)

Denote
\tilde{\beta}_{1}=\omega_{N-1}^{-}a_{N-1}-b_{N-1}+z
,
\tilde{\beta}_{2}=-\omega_{N-1}^{+}a_{N-1}+b_{N-1}-z
such that
\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\beta}_{1}\\
\tilde{\beta}_{2}\end{pmatrix}=(\omega_{N-1}^{-}-\omega_{N-1}^{+})V_{N-1}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{N}\\
\beta_{N-1}\end{pmatrix}
. Hence, we have for
k=1,\dots,N-1

\beta_{k}=\frac{\left((C_{k})_{1,1}+(C_{k})_{2,1}+\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\omega_{l}^{+}\right)\tilde{\beta}_{1}+\left((C_{k})_{1,2}+(C_{k})_{2,2}+\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\omega_{l}^{-}\right)\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\omega_{N-1}^{-}-\omega_{N-1}^{+}}.

(134)

By convention, let
C_{N}\equiv 0
and
\prod_{l=N}^{N-1}\equiv 1
. Then we extend (
134
) to
k=1,\dots,N
, since
\beta_{N}=a_{N-1}
. Use the construction of
M_{j}
in (
88
) to write
V_{k}^{-1}V_{k+1}=M_{k}+\mathrm{Id}
. Then rewrite
C_{k}
via the following equality

A_{k}A_{k+1}\dots A_{N-1}=V_{k}\Omega_{k}(\mathrm{Id}+M_{k})\Omega_{k+1}(\mathrm{Id}+M_{k+1})\dots\Omega_{N-2}(\mathrm{Id}+M_{N-2})\Omega_{N-1}V_{N-1}^{-1}.

(135)

By assumption
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(b_{l}-z)>0
, we have
|\omega_{l}^{+}|\geq|\omega_{l}^{-}|
. Hence,
\|\Omega_{l}\|_{\infty}<|\omega_{l}^{+}|
. Plug (
135
) into (
132
) to estimate

\|C_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\left(\prod_{l=k}^{N-2}\bigg(1+\|M_{l}\|_{\infty}\bigg)-1\right)\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}|\omega_{l}^{+}|.

(136)

Then by (
127
),
0<\varepsilon_{1}<1
and the fact that
(1+x)^{y}<e^{xy}<1+2xy
for all
x,y>0
with
0<xy<1
, we have

\|C_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\varepsilon_{1}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}|\omega_{l}^{+}|.

(137)

Denote
(C_{k})_{j,l}
to be the entry of matrix
C_{k}
at the
j-th
row and
l-th
column. Then let

C(k)\coloneqq\left((C_{k})_{1,1}+(C_{k})_{2,1}\right)\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}(\omega_{l}^{+})^{-1}+\left((C_{k})_{1,2}+(C_{k})_{2,2}\right)\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}(\omega_{l}^{+})^{-1}.

(138)

By (
126
) we have
\left|\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}\right|=c
and thus

|C(k)|\leq 4(1+c)\varepsilon_{1}.

Moreover, use (
138
) to rewrite (
134
) as

\beta_{k}=\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{1}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\omega_{l}^{+}}{\omega_{N-1}^{-}-\omega_{N-1}^{+}}\left(1+\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+C(k)\right).

(139)

Then plug (
139
) into (
130
) to obtain that for
j\leq N_{1}<k

\displaystyle\left(J_{N}^{-1}\right)_{j,k}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k-N_{1}}\left(J_{N}^{-1}\right)_{j,N_{1}}\left(\prod_{l=N_{1}}^{k-1}\omega_{l}^{+}\right)^{-1}\frac{1+\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+C(k)}{1+\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}\prod_{l=N_{1}}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+C(N_{1})}.

(140)

Note that
\left|\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}\right|<1
. Thus whenever
4(1+c)\varepsilon_{1}+c\varepsilon_{2}<1
, we have, by triangle inequality,

\left|\left(J_{N}^{-1}\right)_{j,k}\right|\leq\frac{1+4(1+c)\varepsilon_{1}+c}{1-4(1+c)\varepsilon_{1}-c\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|J_{N}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\prod_{l=N_{1}}^{k-1}\left|\omega_{l}^{+}\right|^{-1}

(141)

∎

5.2
Proof of Proposition
4.1

In this subsection, we use Proposition
5.1
to prove Proposition
4.1
, whose assumptions are the same as Theorem
2.3
.

Let
\beta=\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{3}
such that
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}<1
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
We consider the tri-diagonal matrix
J_{N}
, where

N=n+2mn^{\beta},\quad b_{j}=b_{j,n}-x_{0},\quad a_{j}=a_{j,n},\quad z=\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}},

(142)

where
x_{0}
is near the edge, i.e., (
24
) is assumed. Indeed, we are looking at the case where
J_{N}=J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
as defined as (
93
). Since the subscript
r
of
\eta_{r}
will not play any role in what follows, we suppress it in this section. To consider the case
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
, we will point out at the end of the proof of Proposition
4.1
that the proof is the same up to relabelling of the indices.

Now let

I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}\coloneqq\{j\in\mathbb{N}:N_{0}\leq j\leq N\},\quad\text{where }N_{0}=n-2mn^{\beta},N=n+2mn^{\beta}.

(143)

Note that
I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}\subset I_{n}^{(\alpha,\varepsilon)}
, where
I_{n}^{(\alpha,\varepsilon)}
is defined as (
21
). The size of set
I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
is denoted by
\left|I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}\right|
and is of order
O(n^{\beta})
.

In Theorem
2.3
, our main assumptions are about the coefficients
a_{j,n}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{j,n}\in\mathbb{R}
. For reader’s convenience, we list them in terms of
a_{j}
and
b_{j}
(under the setup (
142
)) as follows.

Condition 5.1

(Theorem
2.3
)
.

There exist some constants
c_{0},c_{1}>0
such that for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
, where
I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
is defined as (
143
), we have

\displaystyle c_{0}<|a_{j}|<c_{1}

\displaystyle,\quad|b_{j}|<c_{1},

(144)

\displaystyle|a_{j}-a_{j-1}|\leq\frac{c_{1}}{n}

\displaystyle,\quad|b_{j}-b_{j-1}|\leq\frac{c_{1}}{n}.

(145)

Condition 5.2

(Theorem
2.3
)
.

As
n\to\infty
,

\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|a_{j}a_{j-2}-a_{j-1}^{2}\right|=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}})

(146)

\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|(b_{j-1}-a_{j})a_{j-2}-(b_{j-2}-a_{j-1})a_{j-1}\right|=o(n^{-3\beta}),\quad\text{for the left edge }.

(147)

\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|b_{j-1}-2\sqrt{a_{j}a_{j-1}}\right|=o(n^{-\alpha}),\quad\text{for the left edge }.

(148)

For the right edge, we should replace
a_{j}
in (
147
) to be
-a_{j}
for all j and replace (
148
) by
b_{j-1}=-2\sqrt{a_{j}a_{j-1}}+o(n^{-\alpha})
. In the rest of this section, we will only consider the left edge. The discussion about the right edge of
\mathcal{J}
is the same as that of the left edge of
-\mathcal{J}
, whose off-diagonals are
-a_{j,n}
which is the only reason we do not assume
a_{j,n}>0
in this paper. Note that by Condition
5.2
, the assumption (
147
) for both left and right edges can be compressed to be
\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|(|b_{j-1}|-a_{j})a_{j-2}-(|b_{j-2}|-a_{j-1})a_{j-1}\right|=o(n^{-3\beta})
.

Also note that the indexing set
I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
is a subset of that in the Theorem
2.3
and hence we make a slightly weaker assumption. The difference is only in reducing the notation in both the statement and the proof.

We continue to explain the implications of these conditions by the following lemmas.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume conditions of Theorem
2.3
are satisfied for the left edge, i.e., given Conditions
5.1
and
5.2
.
Then given
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
, we have,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\min_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}b_{j}>0,

(149)

and as
n\to\infty

\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|b_{j-1}^{2}-4a_{j}a_{j-1}\right|=o(n^{-\alpha}).

(150)

Proof.

Recall that
N
and
N_{0}
are defined in (
143
). Then the size of the set
|I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}|=N-N_{0}=4mn^{\beta}
, with
0<\beta<1
. By (
145
), we have

\min_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}b_{j}\geq b_{n-1}-4c_{1}mn^{\beta-1}.

(151)

By (
148
), we have

b_{n-1}=2\sqrt{a_{n-1}a_{n}}+o(n^{-\alpha}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(152)

Moreover, by (
148
) and the lower bound of
|a_{j}|
in (
144
), we have
\sqrt{a_{n-1}a_{n}}>2c_{0}>0
. Consequently, we have (
149
).

Now we prove the second statement. By (
147
), together with the lower bound of
a_{j}
in Condition
5.2
, we have that for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}

\left(\frac{b_{j-1}-a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}\right)-\left(\frac{b_{j-2}-a_{j-1}}{a_{j-2}}\right)=o(n^{-3\beta}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(153)

Note that the size of the index set
|I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}|=O(n^{\beta})
. Then for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}

\left(\frac{b_{j-1}-a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}\right)-\left(\frac{b_{n-1}-a_{n}}{a_{n-1}}\right)=o\left(n^{-2\beta}\right),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(154)

Using the fact that
\left(\sqrt{\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}}-1\right)^{2}=\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}+1-2\sqrt{\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}}
, we can rewrite for
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}

\frac{b_{j-1}-a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}=\frac{b_{j-1}}{a_{j-1}}-\left(\sqrt{\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-2}}}-1\right)^{2}-2\sqrt{\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}}+1.

(155)

Note that by the Condition
5.1
we have
\left(\sqrt{\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}}-1\right)^{2}=O(n^{-2})
. Also note that (
155
) holds for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
including the special case
j=n
.
Hence plugging (
155
) into (
154
) gives

\frac{b_{j-1}}{a_{j-1}}-2\sqrt{\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}}-\frac{b_{n-1}}{a_{n-1}}+2\sqrt{\frac{a_{n}}{a_{n-1}}}=o(n^{-2\beta})+O(n^{-2}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(156)

By the assumption (
148
) in Condition
5.2
, we have
\frac{b_{n-1}}{a_{n-1}}-2\sqrt{\frac{a_{n}}{a_{n-1}}}=o(n^{-\alpha})
, as
n\to\infty
. Further, by the assumption
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
, we estimate (
156
) to be

\frac{b_{j-1}}{a_{j-1}}-2\sqrt{\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}}=o(n^{-\alpha}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(157)

Multiply
\frac{b_{j-1}}{a_{j-1}}+2\sqrt{\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}}
on both sides of (
157
) to obtain that for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}

\left(\frac{b_{j-1}}{a_{j-1}}\right)^{2}=\frac{4a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}+o(n^{-\alpha}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty,

(158)

which completes the proof.
∎

Note that (
149
) implies that
|\omega_{j}^{-}|\leq|\omega_{j}^{+}|
, since we are taking the principal square root. However, for the right edge, by the same argument, we have
b_{j}<0
for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
. In this case, we can swap the definition of
\omega_{j}^{+}
and
\omega_{j}^{-}
and all conclusions in this section follow with the same argument. Alternatively, for the right edge, we can simply consider
-J_{N}
whose diagonals are
-b_{j}+\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}
and off diagonals are
-a_{j}
. Hence, all discussions in this section can be directly applied to
(-J_{N})^{-1}
, since
-b_{j}>0
for the right edge.

Lemma 5.2
.

Consider
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
. Assume the Conditions
5.1
and
5.2
are satisfied. Then we have

\displaystyle\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|\omega_{j}^{-}-\left(1-\left(\frac{-\eta}{n^{\alpha}|a_{N-1}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)sgn(a_{N-1})\right|=o(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}),

(159)

\displaystyle\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|\omega_{j}^{+}-\left(1+\left(\frac{-\eta}{n^{\alpha}|a_{N-1}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)sgn(a_{N-1})\right|=o(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}),

(160)

where
sgn(x)=1
if
x\geq 0
and
sgn(x)=-1
if
x<0
. The choice of
a_{N-1}
in the formulas above is arbitrary. It can be replaced by any
a_{j}
for
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\omega_{j}^{-}
is given by

\displaystyle\omega_{j}^{-}=\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}-\left(\left(b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{2}-4a_{j-1}a_{j}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2a_{j-1}}.

(161)

By Condition
5.1
we have
\frac{a_{l}}{a_{l-1}}=1+O(n^{-1})
. Further, by Lemma
5.1
, we obtain

\left(\frac{b_{j-1}}{2a_{j-1}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}=o(n^{-\alpha}),\quad\frac{b_{j-1}}{2|a_{j-1}|}=1+o(n^{-\alpha})+O(n^{-1}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(162)

Condition
5.1
also implies
a_{j-1}=a_{N-1}+O(n^{\beta-1})
. We can then further estimate as
n\to\infty

\left(\left(\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-1}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(\left(\frac{b_{j-1}}{2a_{j-1}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}-\frac{b_{j-1}}{2a_{j-1}}\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}a_{j-1}}+\left(\frac{\eta}{2n^{\alpha}a_{j-1}}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
=\left(o(n^{-\alpha})-\left(1+o(n^{-\alpha})+O(n^{-1})\right)\frac{\eta}{2n^{\alpha}\left(|a_{N-1}|+O(n^{\beta-1})\right)}+O(n^{2\alpha})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(\frac{-\eta}{n^{\alpha}|a_{N-1}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+o(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}).

Hence we proved (
159
) for
\omega_{j}^{-}
. The proof for
\omega_{j}^{+}
follows from the same argument.
∎

Note that for the right edge where
b_{j}<0
, we will get the following by the same argument,

\displaystyle\omega_{j}^{-}=\left(-1-\left(\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}|a_{N-1}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)sgn(a_{N-1})+o(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}),

(163)

\displaystyle\omega_{j}^{+}=\left(-1+\left(\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}|a_{N-1}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)sgn(a_{N-1})+o(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}).

(164)

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}<1
.
Assume Conditions
5.1
, and
5.2
are satisfied. Then we have as
n\to\infty

\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|\omega_{j}^{+}-\omega_{j-1}^{+}\right|=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}),\quad\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|\omega_{j}^{-}-\omega_{j-1}^{-}\right|=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}).

(165)

Consequently we also have as
n\to\infty

\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\|M_{j}\|_{\infty}=o(n^{-3\beta+\alpha}).

(166)

Proof.

First note that the difference of equation (
146
) and (
147
) amounts to

\max_{j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\left|b_{j-1}a_{j-2}-b_{j-2}a_{j-1}\right|=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(167)

Recall, from the definition (
87
), we have
\omega_{j}^{+}-\omega_{j}^{-}=\frac{\left((b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}})^{2}-4a_{j}a_{j-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{a_{j-1}}
, where the square root is taken as the principal branch. By Lemma
5.1
and (
144
) we have
|\omega_{j}^{+}-\omega_{j}^{-}|^{-1}=O(n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
. Moreover,

\omega_{j}^{+}-\omega_{j-1}^{+}=\frac{b_{j-1}}{2a_{j-1}}-\frac{b_{j-2}}{2a_{j-2}}-\frac{\eta}{2n^{\alpha}}\left(\frac{1}{a_{j-1}}-\frac{1}{a_{j-2}}\right)+\left(\left(\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-1}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(\left(\frac{b_{j-2}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j-1}}{a_{j-2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The first part can be estimated as
\frac{b_{j-1}}{2a_{j-1}}-\frac{b_{j-2}}{2a_{j-2}}=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}})
, by (
167
). The second part can be estimated as
\frac{\eta}{2n^{\alpha}}\left(\frac{1}{a_{j-1}}-\frac{1}{a_{j-2}}\right)=O(n^{-\alpha-1})
, by Condition
5.1
. For the third part, we have

\left(\left(\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-1}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(\left(\frac{b_{j-2}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j-1}}{a_{j-2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
=\frac{\left(\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-1}}-\frac{b_{j-2}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-2}}\right)\left(\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-1}}+\frac{b_{j-2}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-2}}\right)-\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}+\frac{a_{j-1}}{a_{j-2}}}{\left(\left(\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-1}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\left(\frac{b_{j-2}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a_{j-1}}{a_{j-2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(168)

By the same argument as before, we have
1
over the denominator is also of order
O(n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
. For the numerator, Lemma
5.1
together with (
144
) implies that
\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-1}}=\left|\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+O(n^{-\alpha})
. Moreover, Condition
5.1
implies
\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}=1+O(n^{-1})
. Since
\alpha\in(0,2)
, we have
\frac{b_{j-1}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-1}}+\frac{b_{j-2}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}}{2a_{j-2}}=2+O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})
. The numerator of (
168
) now becomes

\frac{b_{j-1}-a_{j}}{a_{j-1}}-\frac{b_{j-2}-a_{j-1}}{a_{j-2}}-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\left(\frac{1}{a_{j-1}}-\frac{1}{a_{j-2}}\right)+\left(\frac{b_{j-1}}{a_{j-1}}-\frac{b_{j-2}}{a_{j-2}}\right)O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}).

(169)

The sum of the first two terms is
o(n^{-3\beta})
by (
147
). The third term is order
O(n^{-\alpha-1})
, by Condition
5.1
. For the rest term, we have
\frac{b_{j-1}}{a_{j-1}}-\frac{b_{j-2}}{a_{j-2}}=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}})
, by (
167
). Then (
169
) is of order
o(n^{-3\beta})
, by the assumption
\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}
. Combining the arguments above, we have
\omega_{j}^{+}-\omega_{j-1}^{+}=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}})
. Similarly, we also have
\omega_{j}^{-}-\omega_{j-1}^{-}=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}})
. Then by the definition of
M_{j}
(
88
), we have
\|M_{j}\|_{\infty}=o(n^{-3\beta+\alpha})
.
∎

Now we are ready to verify the Proposition
4.1
. Recall that
\mathcal{J}
is the Jacobi matrix with entries
\{a_{j,n},b_{j,n}\}_{j}
. The truncated operators
J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
,
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
,
F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}
and
F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
are defined in (
93
), (
94
) and (
95
).

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

First, consider

J_{N}=J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}.

It is sufficient to verify the conditions in Proposition
5.1
.

For all
l\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
, by Lemma
5.1
we have
b_{l}>0
. By Lemma
5.2
we have
\omega_{l}^{-}=1+O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})
and
\omega_{l}^{-}=1+O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})
. Hence

\left|\frac{-\omega_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}^{+}a_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}+b_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}-z}{\omega_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}^{-}a_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}-b_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}+z}\right|=1+O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(170)

This shows (
126
) is bounded.

Let
M_{j}
be defined as (
88
). By Lemma
5.3
we have as
n\to\infty

\left|I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}\right|\max_{l\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\|M_{l}\|_{\infty}=o(n^{-2\beta+\alpha})

(171)

This shows (
127
) to be
\varepsilon_{1}=o(n^{-2\beta+\alpha})
. Moreover, use Lemma
5.2
to estimate that there exist constants
d>0
and
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
n>n_{0}
and all integer
N_{1}
with
N_{0}\leq N_{1}<N

\prod_{l=N_{1}}^{N-1}\left|\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}\right|\leq\left(1-\frac{d}{n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right)^{N-N_{1}}\leq e^{-dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(N-N_{1})}.

(172)

This shows (
128
) to be
\varepsilon_{2}=O(e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}})
which is exponentially small.

Then all assumptions of Proposition
5.1
are cleared. Hence, for all
j\leq N_{1}<k
we have

\left|\left(J_{N}^{-1}\right)_{j,k}\right|\leq c\left\|J_{N}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}e^{-dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(k-N_{1})}.

(173)

for some constant
c>0
. Then take
N_{1}=k-n^{\beta}\geq N_{0}
to obtain (
96
).

Note that above argument is taken as
b_{j}>0
as assumed in (
149
). For
b_{j}<0
, we consider
-J_{N}
whose diagonals are
-b_{j}+\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}
and off diagonals are
-a_{j}
. Apply the result above, and we reach the same estimate for
-J_{N}^{-1}
.

Note that the entry
\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)_{j,k}
is the same as
\left(J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)_{j,k}
for all
j,k\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
and the rest of the entries are in the trivial blocks. Hence, the result (
97
) for
J_{N}=J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
follows the same approach by relabelling the indices appropriately.

Then, the estimates (
98
) and (
99
) follow from the definition of
F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}
and
F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
in (
93
) and (
95
) and triangle inequality.
∎

5.3
Comparison in the Trace Norm

The following lemma is essential to prove Proposition
4.2
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Assume conditions in Proposition
4.2
are satisfied. Then for any
l\leq m
and
n>n_{0}
for some constant
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|Q_{n+ln^{\beta}}F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{l}P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(174)

\displaystyle\left\|Q_{n+ln^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l}P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(175)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(n^{\alpha l}\left(f_{\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\left(\mathcal{J}\right)\right)^{(l)}-F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{l}\right)P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(176)

where
C_{m}>0
is a constant that depends on
m
and
d^{\prime}>0
is also an universal constant.

Proof.

To shorten the notation in the proof, we take

N=n+2mn^{\beta}.

Recall the trace norm inequality
\|A\|_{1}\leq\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}|(A)_{k_{1},k_{2}}|
for any matrix
A
. By Proposition
4.1
, we have

\left\|Q_{n+n^{\beta}}F_{N}P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j=n+n^{\beta}+1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{n}C_{0}n^{1+\alpha}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}=C_{0}(2m-1)n^{2+\alpha+\beta}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(177)

similarly,

\left\|Q_{n+(l+1)n^{\beta}}F_{N}P_{n+ln^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j=n+(l+1)n^{\beta}+1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{n+ln^{\beta}}C_{0}n^{1+\alpha}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}=C_{0}(2m-l-1)n^{1+\alpha+\beta}(n+ln^{\beta})e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(178)

We will prove the following statement for all
l\leq m
by induction:

\displaystyle\left\|Q_{n+ln^{\beta}}F_{N}^{l}P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}(2m-1)ln^{1+\alpha+\beta}(n+mn^{\beta})\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}^{l-1}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(179)

For
l=1
the statement follows by (
177
). Then write
F_{N}^{l+1}=F_{N}(P_{n+ln^{\beta}}+Q_{n+ln^{\beta}})F_{N}^{l}
. With the triangle inequality and trace norm inequality
\|AB\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{1}\|B\|_{\infty}
, we get

\left\|Q_{n+(l+1)n^{\beta}}F_{N}^{l+1}P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|Q_{n+(l+1)n^{\beta}}F_{N}P_{n+ln^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\|F_{N}\|^{l}_{\infty}+\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}\left\|Q_{n+ln^{\beta}}F_{N}^{l}P_{n}\right\|_{1}.

(180)

Then apply (
178
) to the first trace norm and the induction hypothesis (
179
) for the second trace norm above, and we obtain

\left\|Q_{n+(l+1)n^{\beta}}F_{N}^{l+1}P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq C_{0}(2m-1)(l+1)n^{1+\alpha+\beta}(n+mn^{\beta})\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}^{l}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(181)

This concludes the induction.

Moreover, since
\mathcal{J}
is real symmetric and
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\eta)/n^{\alpha}\neq 0
, we have
\left\|\left(J_{N}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{n^{\alpha}}{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\eta_{r})|}
. Hence
\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{r}\left|\frac{c_{r}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\eta_{r})}\right|n^{\alpha}
. By inserting this in (
179
), we see (
174
) holds.

The second statement (
174
) follows in the same way, since Proposition
4.1
also holds for
F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
.

Now we prove the third statement (
176
). By Lemma
3.5
we have, for
l=1,2,\dots
,

\left\|n^{\alpha l}P_{n}\left(f_{\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\left(\mathcal{J}\right)\right)^{(l)}-P_{n}F_{N}^{l}\right\|_{1}\leq\left|a_{N,n}\right|\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}n^{\alpha(l-1-j)}\sum_{r=1}^{M}\left\|P_{n}F_{N}^{j}\left(J_{N}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}E_{N}\right\|_{1}\left\|B_{r,n}^{(l-1-j)}\right\|_{\infty}.

(182)

where
E_{N}
is a semi-infinite matrix with only one entry non-zero
(E_{N})_{i,j}\coloneqq\mathrm{1}_{i=N,j=N+1}
. We further estimate the trace norm in (
182
) to be

\left\|P_{n}F_{N}^{j}\left(J_{N}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}E_{N}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|P_{n}F_{N}^{j}P_{n+ln^{\beta}}\left(J_{N}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}E_{N}\right\|_{1}+\left\|P_{n}F_{N}^{j}Q_{n+ln^{\beta}}\left(J_{N}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}E_{N}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\left\|F_{N}\right\|_{\infty}^{j}\left\|P_{n+ln^{\beta}}\left(J_{N}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}E_{N}\right\|_{1}+\left\|P_{n}F_{N}^{j}Q_{n+ln^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\left\|\left(J_{N}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}.

(183)

By Proposition
4.1
and trace norm estimate, we have the first trace norm in (
183
) to be
\left\|P_{n+ln^{\beta}}\left(J_{N}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}E_{N}\right\|_{1}\leq C_{0}(n+ln^{\beta})n^{\alpha}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}
. We also estimate the second trace norm in (
183
) to be exponentially small by (
179
). Note that the operator norms in (
183
) are of order
n^{\alpha}
. Hence, the lemma is concluded.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then for any
l\leq m
we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{n-ln^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l}Q_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}

(184)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{l}-F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l}\right)Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}

(185)

where
a_{n-2mn^{\beta}}
is the
n-2mn^{\beta}+1,n-2mn^{\beta}
-th entry of
\mathcal{J}
,
C_{m}>0
is a constant that depends on
m
and
d^{\prime}>0
is also a universal constant.

Proof.

This proof follows the same recipe as the one of Lemma
5.4
. Using the trace norm inequality
\|A\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j,k}|(A)_{j,k}|
for any matrix
A
and applying the second statement of Proposition
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|P_{n-n^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}Q_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j=n-2mn^{\beta}+1}^{n-(1+m)n^{\beta}}\sum_{k=n-mn^{\beta}+1}^{n+2mn^{\beta}}C_{0}n^{1+\alpha}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}=C_{0}(2m-1)2mn^{1+\alpha+2\beta}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(186)

similarly,

\left\|P_{n-(l+1)n^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-ln^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j=n-2mn^{\beta}+1}^{n-(l+1)n^{\beta}}\sum_{k=n-ln^{\beta}+1}^{n+2mn^{\beta}}C_{0}n^{1+\alpha}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\\
=C_{0}(2m-l-1)(2m+l)n^{1+\alpha+2\beta}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(187)

We will prove the following statement for all
l\leq m
by induction:

\|P_{n-ln^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l}Q_{n}\|_{1}\leq 3C_{0}(2m-1)mn^{1+\alpha+2\beta}l\|F_{n\pm mn^{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{l-1}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(188)

For
l=1
the statement follows by (
186
). Then write
F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l+1}=F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(P_{n+ln^{\beta}}+Q_{n+ln^{\beta}})F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l}
. With the triangle inequality and trace norm inequality
\|AB\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{1}\|B\|_{\infty}
, we get

\left\|P_{n-(l+1)n^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l+1}Q_{n}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\left\|P_{n-(l+1)n^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-ln^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\|F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\|^{l}_{\infty}+\|F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\|_{\infty}\left\|P_{n-ln^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}Q_{n}\right\|_{1}

(189)

Then apply (
187
) to the first trace norm and the induction hypothesis (
188
) for the second trace norm above, and we obtain

\left\|P_{n-(l+1)n^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l+1}Q_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq 3C_{0}(2m-1)mn^{1+\alpha+2\beta}(l+1)\|F_{n\pm mn^{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{l}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(190)

This concludes the induction.

For the second statement, to shorten the notation, let us denote

\tilde{N}=n-2mn^{\beta}.

Let

\displaystyle E\coloneqq J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}-P_{\tilde{N}}J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}P_{\tilde{N}}-Q_{\tilde{N}}J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}Q_{\tilde{N}}.

(191)

Note that
E
is independent of
r
. It is because that
E
is a semi-infinite matrix with only two entries non zero, i.e.
(E)_{\tilde{N}+1,\tilde{N}}=(E)_{\tilde{N},\tilde{N}+1}=a_{\tilde{N}}
. This also implies that
E=EP_{\tilde{N}+1}Q_{\tilde{N}-1}
. Moreover,
\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-l}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}=\left(J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}-E\right)^{-l}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}
.

Hence

F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}-F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}=\sum_{r}c_{r}\left(J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}\left(-EP_{\tilde{N}+1}Q_{\tilde{N}-1}-P_{\tilde{N}}J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}P_{\tilde{N}}\right)\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}.

Further by telescopic sum, we have

\left(F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{l}-F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l}\right)Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}=-\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}\sum_{r}F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{j}c_{r}\left(J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}EP_{\tilde{N}+1}Q_{\tilde{N}-1}\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l-j-1}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}},

which follows from
P_{\tilde{N}}\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l-j-1}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}=0
.

By triangle inequality and trace norm inequality, we have

\left\|\left(F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{l}-F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l}\right)Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}\sum_{r}|c_{r}|\|F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{j}\left\|\left(J_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\|E\|_{\infty}\left\|P_{\tilde{N}+1}Q_{\tilde{N}-1}\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l-j-1}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}.

(192)

Write
\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}=\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}\left(P_{n-(l-j-1+m)n^{\beta}}+Q_{n-(l-j-1+m)n^{\beta}}\right)
, use triangle inequality and we have

\left\|P_{\tilde{N}+1}Q_{\tilde{N}-1}\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l-j-1}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|P_{\tilde{N}+1}Q_{\tilde{N}-1}\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}Q_{n-(l-j-1+m)n^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\left\|F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{\infty}^{l-j-1}+\tilde{R}_{l-j-1}

where
\tilde{R}_{l-j-1}=\left\|\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|P_{n-(l-j-1+m)n^{\beta}}F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l-j-1}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}
. Note that
\tilde{R}_{0}=0
. For
j<l-1
we can estimate
\tilde{R}_{l-j-1}
via (
188
) to be

\tilde{R}_{l-j-1}\leq 4C_{0}(m-1)mn^{1+\alpha+2\beta}(l-j-1)\|J_{n\pm mn^{\beta}}^{-1}\|_{\infty}^{l-j-1}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

Further using the trace norm inequality
\|A\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j,k}|(A)_{j,k}|
for any matrix
A
and estimating the entries via Proposition
4.1
, we have

\left\|P_{\tilde{N}+1}Q_{\tilde{N}-1}J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{-1}Q_{n-(l-j-1+m)n^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\leq\sum_{k=n-(l-j-1+m)n^{\beta}+1}^{n+2mn^{\beta}}C_{0}n^{1+\alpha}n^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\leq C_{0}(3m+l)n^{1+\alpha+\beta}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

Assembling the above estimates into (
192
), we have for all
l\leq m

\displaystyle\left\|\left(F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}^{l}-F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{l}\right)Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\leq\|E\|_{\infty}C_{m}^{\prime}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}

where
d^{\prime}\in(0,d_{0})
and
C_{m}^{\prime}>0
some universal constant. Note that
\|E\|_{\infty}=|a_{\tilde{N}}|<c_{1}
. This concludes the lemma.
∎

5.4
Proof of Proposition
4.2

Recall that for any linear operator
\mathcal{A}
we write by (
64
) for any
m\geq 2

\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(\mathcal{A})=m!\sum_{j=2}^{m}\frac{(-1)^{j+1}}{j}\sum_{l_{1}+\dots+l_{j}=m,l_{i}\geq 1}\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{A})^{l_{1}}P_{n}\dots(\mathcal{A})^{l_{j}}P_{n}-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n})}{l_{1}!\dots l_{j}!}.

We first explore some algebraic properties of the trace above.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
m\geq 2
,
j=2,\dots,m
and
l_{i}\geq 1
with
l_{1}+\dots+l_{j}=m
. Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be bounded linear operators. Let
P_{n}
be the projection operator onto the first
n
coordinates and
Q_{n}=\mathrm{Id}-P_{n}
. Then

\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n}\right)\right)-\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{B}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{m}P_{n}\right)\right)\\
=-\sum_{k=2}^{j}\Bigg(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left((\mathcal{A}^{l_{k}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}})P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{k+1}}\cdots P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}Q_{n}\right)\\
+\sum_{i=k}^{j-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}}P_{n}\cdots P_{n}\mathcal{B}^{l_{i}}P_{n}(\mathcal{A}^{l_{i+1}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{i+1}})P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}Q_{n}\right)\\
+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}}P_{n}\cdots P_{n}\mathcal{B}^{l_{j}}P_{n}(\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}})Q_{n}\right)\Bigg),

(193)

here we take the convention that
\sum_{j=a}^{b}\equiv 0
for any integers
b<a
.

Proof.

We write

\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n}=\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}}(P_{n}+Q_{n})\mathcal{A}^{l_{2}}(P_{n}+Q_{n})\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}.

By expanding the formula above, we find

\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}-\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n}\\
=-\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}}Q_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{2}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}-\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+l_{2}}Q_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{3}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}-\cdots-\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{j-1}}Q_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}.

(194)

Using the cyclic property of the trace, we obtain

\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n}\right)=-\sum_{k=2}^{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{l_{k}}P_{n}\cdots P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}Q_{n}\right).

(195)

Then by (
195
), we further write

\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n}\right)\right)-\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{B}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{m}P_{n}\right)\right)\\
=\sum_{k=2}^{j}-Tr\left(\mathcal{A}^{l_{k}}P_{n}\cdots P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}Q_{n}\right)+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}}P_{n}\cdots P_{n}\mathcal{B}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\mathcal{B}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}Q_{n}\right)

(196)

Similarly, using the telescoping sum and cyclic property of the trace operator, we conclude (
193
).
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
m\geq 2
,
j=2,\dots,m
and
l_{i}\geq 1
with
l_{1}+\dots+l_{j}=m
. Take
\mathcal{A}=F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
and
\mathcal{B}
be any bounded operator. Assume there exists a constant
C_{op}>1
such that

\max\{\|\mathcal{A}\|_{\infty},\|\mathcal{B}\|_{\infty}\}\leq n^{\alpha}C_{op}.

(197)

Also assume for any
l\leq m

\left\|P_{n}\mathcal{B}^{l}Q_{n+ln^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\leq C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(198)

Define

R_{n}\coloneqq 2mC_{m}\left(n^{\alpha}C_{op}\right)^{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(199)

where
C_{m}
is the same constant in the upper bound in Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5
.
Let
P_{n}
be the projection operator onto the first
n
coordinates and
Q_{n}=\mathrm{Id}-P_{n}
. We have

\left|\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n}\right)\right)-\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots\mathcal{B}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{m}P_{n}\right)\right)\right|\\
\leq(m+1)\left(\left(n^{\alpha}C_{op}\right)^{m+1}\left\|(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}+R_{n}\right).

(200)

Proof.

Apply (
175
) in Lemma
5.4
, and apply the trace norm inequality,
\|AB\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}\|B\|_{1}
, and the fact that, for
i<m
to obtain

\|Q_{n+mn^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\|_{1}=\|Q_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n+in^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\|_{1}\leq\|Q_{n+mn^{\beta}}\|_{\infty}\|Q_{n+in^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(201)

Use the fact
\mathcal{A}^{l_{k}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}}=\sum_{i=0}^{l_{k}-1}\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}-1-i}(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})\mathcal{A}^{i}
, together with (
201
), to obtain

\left\|\left(\mathcal{A}^{l_{k}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}}\right)P_{n}-\sum_{i=0}^{l_{k}-1}\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}-1-i}(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\right\|_{1}=\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{l_{k}-1}\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}-1-i}(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})Q_{n+mn^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\sum_{i=0}^{l_{k}-1}\left\|\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}-1-i}(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})\right\|_{\infty}\|Q_{n+mn^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\|_{1}\leq 2l_{k}\left(n^{\alpha}C_{op}\right)^{l_{k}-i}C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(202)

To shorten the notation for the rest of the proof, we define

\mathcal{V}^{[k]}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k}}Q_{n},\qquad\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k,j]}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{l_{k}}P_{n}\cdots P_{n}\mathcal{A}^{l_{j}}P_{n},\text{ for $k\leq j$,}\quad\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k,j]}\equiv 1\text{ for $k>j$}.

(203)

Then, for
k=2,\dots,j-1
, use (
202
) to estimate the first summand in (
193
) to be

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left((\mathcal{A}^{l_{k}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}})P_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}\right)\right|\leq\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{l_{k}-1}\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}-1-i}(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}\right\|_{1}+R_{n}.

(204)

We write
P_{n}=\mathrm{Id}-Q_{n}
, use the telescopic sum, and get

\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}=-\mathcal{A}^{i}Q_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}-\sum_{p=k+1}^{j}\mathcal{A}^{i+l_{k+1}+\cdots+l_{p}}Q_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[p+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}+\mathcal{A}^{i+m-l_{k}}Q_{n}.

(205)

Note that there are
j-k+2
terms on the right-hand side of (
205
) and
j-k+2\leq m
.

By (
184
) in Lemma
5.5
we have, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
with
l\leq m
,

\|\mathcal{A}^{l}Q_{n}-Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{l}Q_{n}\|_{1}=\|P_{n-mn^{\beta}}P_{n-ln^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{l}Q_{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(206)

Hence, estimate (
205
) by (
206
) to obtain

\left\|\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}-Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}\right\|_{1}\leq(j-k+2)C_{m}\left(n^{\alpha}C_{op}\right)^{m-l_{k}-i}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(207)

Plug (
207
) into the right-hand side of (
204
) to obtain

\left\|\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}-1-i}(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{i}P_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}\right\|_{1}\leq\left(n^{\alpha}C_{op}\right)^{m-1}\|(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}+R_{n}.

(208)

Plug (
208
) into the right-hand side of (
204
), and we get

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left((\mathcal{A}^{l_{k}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}})P_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}\right)\right|\leq l_{k}(n^{\alpha}C_{op})^{m-1}\|(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}+(l_{k}+1)R_{n}.

(209)

Now we estimate the second summand in (
193
). Define
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{B}}^{[k,j]}
analogously as (
203
). For
i=k,\dots,j-1
,

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{B}}^{[k,i]}(\mathcal{A}^{l_{i+1}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{i+1}})P_{n}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{[k+1,j]}\mathcal{V}^{[k-1]}\right)\right|\leq l_{i+1}(n^{\alpha}C_{op})^{m-1}\|(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}+(l_{i+1}+1)R_{n}.

(210)

Lastly, we estimate the last summand in (
193
).

By (
198
) we have

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{B}}^{[k,j]}(\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}})Q_{n}\right)\right|\leq\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{B}}^{[k,j]}(\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n}\right)\right|+R_{n}.

(211)

Repeat the argument similar to (
209
) to obtain

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{B}}^{[k,j]}(\mathcal{A}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1}})Q_{n}\right)\right|\\
\leq(l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1})(n^{\alpha}C_{op})^{m-1}\|(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}+((l_{1}+\cdots+l_{k-1})+1)R_{n}.

(212)

Hence, plugging three estimates (
209
), (
210
) and (
212
) into the formula (
193
), we conclude (
200
).
∎

Now we are ready to prove the Proposition
4.2
by Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5
.

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

Step 1 Consider
(F)^{(l)}\coloneqq n^{\alpha l}\left(f_{\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}\left(\mathcal{J}\right)\right)^{(l)}
as in (
46
) and
\mathcal{B}=F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}
.

By Lemma
5.4
we have for any
l\leq m
we have
\left\|\left((F)^{(l)}-\mathcal{B}^{l}\right)P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}
. Note that we have
\|(F)^{(l)}\|_{\infty},\|\mathcal{B}^{l}\|_{\infty}\leq\left(\sum_{r}\left|\frac{c_{r}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\eta_{r})}\right|n^{\alpha}\right)^{l}
. Then use the telescopic sum and the trace norm inequality to obtain

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(F)^{(l_{1})}P_{n}\cdots(F)^{(l_{j})}P_{n}-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}((F)^{(m)}P_{n})-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{B})^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots(\mathcal{B})^{l_{j}}P_{n}+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{B}^{m}P_{n})\right|\\
\leq\sum_{k=1}^{j}\left\|\left((F)^{(l_{k})}-\mathcal{B}^{l_{k}}\right)P_{n}\right\|_{1}\left(\sum_{r}\left|\frac{c_{r}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\eta_{r})}\right|n^{\alpha}\right)^{m-l_{k}}+\left\|\left((F)^{(m)}-\mathcal{B}^{m}\right)P_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq C^{\prime\prime}e^{-d^{\prime\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(213)

for some constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0,d^{\prime\prime}>0
. Hence plug the estimate (
213
) into the cumulant formula (
64
) to get

\left|n^{\alpha m}\mathcal{C}_{m}(X^{(n)}_{f,\alpha,x_{0}})-\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(F_{n+2mn^{\beta}})\right|\leq C_{m}^{\prime\prime}e^{-d^{\prime\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(214)

Step 2 Consider
\mathcal{A}=F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
and
\mathcal{B}=F_{n+2mn^{\beta}}

Then by Lemma
5.7
and (
185
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{A})^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots(\mathcal{A})^{l_{j}}P_{n}-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n})-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{B})^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots(\mathcal{B})^{l_{j}}P_{n}+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{B}^{m}P_{n})\right|\leq C^{\prime\prime}e^{-d^{\prime\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(215)

for some constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0,d^{\prime\prime}>0
. Hence plug the estimate (
215
) into the definition (
64
) and we have

\left|\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(F_{n+2mn^{\beta}})-\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}})\right|\leq C_{m}^{\prime\prime}e^{-d^{\prime\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(216)

Combining (
214
) from Step
1
and (
216
) from Step
2
, we conclude (
100
) in Proposition
4.2
.
∎

6
Proof of Propositions
4.3
and
4.4

In this section, we prove Propositions
4.3
and
4.4
. Essentially, we are studying the inverse of the blocked operator
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
defined in (
94
), with
\beta=\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{3}
. It is the middle block,

P_{n+2mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-2mn^{\beta}}J^{(r)}Q_{n-2mn^{\beta}}P_{n+2mn^{\beta}}
,
that is non-trivial and matters in the analysis.

In Section
6.1
, we will compute the resolvent for a general
N\times N
matrix with
N\in\mathbb{N}
, (cf.Proposition
6.1
). Later, in Sections
6.2
and
6.3
, we will apply Proposition
6.1
to the middle block the resolvent of
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
, by taking the size of the matrix to be
4mn^{\beta}
with relabelling of the indexes.

6.1
Inverse of a Tri-diagonal Matrix: Part II

Consider an
N\times N
non singular symmetric matrix with
a_{j}\in\mathbb{R},b_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z\neq 0
.

\displaystyle J_{N}=\begin{pmatrix}b_{0}&a_{1}&&&&&\\
a_{1}&b_{1}&a_{2}&&&&\\
&a_{2}&b_{2}&a_{3}&&&\\
&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots\\
&&&a_{N-2}&b_{N-2}&a_{N-1}\\
&&&&a_{N-1}&b_{N-1}\end{pmatrix}-z\mathrm{Id}

(217)

The goal of this subsection is to continue to develop a theory to estimate the inverse of
J_{N}
entry-wise by imposing stronger assumptions than in Section
5
.

Proposition 6.1

(Almost Toeplitz)
.

Consider an
N\times N
non singular symmetric matrix
J_{N}
with
a_{j}\in\mathbb{R},b_{j}-\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
as defined as (
217
). Let
\omega_{j}^{+},\omega_{j}^{-}
as defined as (
87
). Let
M_{j}
as defined as (
88
). Define

c_{0}=\min_{j}|a_{j}|,\quad c_{1}=\max\left\{\max_{j}|a_{j}|,\max_{j}\left|b_{j-1}-z\right|\right\},

(218)

c_{2}=\max\left\{1,\left|\frac{-a_{1}+\omega_{2}^{-}(b_{0}-z)}{a_{1}-\omega_{2}^{+}(b_{0}-z)}\right|,\left|\frac{-\omega_{N-1}^{+}a_{N-1}+b_{N-1}-z}{\omega_{N-1}^{-}a_{N-1}-b_{N-1}+z}\right|\right\},

(219)

\varepsilon_{1}=N\max_{j}\|M_{j}\|_{\infty},

(220)

\varepsilon_{2}=\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\left|\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}\right|.

(221)

Assume that
c_{0}>0
, and both
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
are sufficiently small.
1
1

1

More precisely, we assume
0<\varepsilon_{1}<3^{-1}\left(\frac{(1+\sqrt{5})c_{1}}{2c_{0}}\right)^{-2},0<\varepsilon_{2}
and

12\left(1+\left(\frac{(1+\sqrt{5})c_{1}}{2c_{0}}\right)^{2}c_{2}^{2}\right)\varepsilon_{1}+c_{2}\varepsilon_{2}<\left(\frac{(1+\sqrt{5})c_{1}}{2c_{0}}\right)^{-2}
.

Then we have

\displaystyle J_{N}^{-1}=T_{N}(\eta)+H_{N}(\eta),

(222)

such that for all
j\leq k

\displaystyle\left|(T_{N}(\eta))_{j,k}\right|\leq

\displaystyle Constant\left|\frac{\prod_{l=j}^{k-1}\omega_{l}^{-}}{a_{k-1}\omega_{j}^{-}\left(\omega_{N-1}^{-}-\omega_{N-1}^{+}\right)}\right|,

(223)

\displaystyle\left|(H_{N}(\eta))_{j,k}\right|\leq

\displaystyle Constant\left|\frac{\prod_{l=j}^{k-1}\omega_{l}^{-}\left(\prod_{l=2}^{j}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}\right)}{a_{k-1}\omega_{j}^{-}\left(\omega_{N-1}^{-}-\omega_{N-1}^{+}\right)}\right|,

(224)

where
Constant
is given by (
254
) that only depends on
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2},\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
and remains bounded as
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\to 0
.

Further we have for any
j,k,l
, as
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\to 0
,

(T_{N}(\eta))_{j,k}=\frac{(-1)^{k-j}\omega_{N-1}^{-}}{\omega_{N-1}^{+}-\omega_{N-1}^{-}}\frac{\prod_{m=j}^{k-1}\omega_{m}^{-}}{a_{k-1}\omega_{j}^{-}}(1+O(\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{2}))

(225)

and

\frac{(T_{N}(\eta))_{j,k}}{(T_{N}(\eta))_{j+l,k+l}}=\left(\prod_{m=j}^{k-1}\frac{\omega_{m}^{-}}{\omega_{m+l}^{-}}\right)\frac{\omega_{l+j}^{-}a_{k+l-1}}{\omega_{j}^{-}a_{k-1}}\left(1+O(\varepsilon_{1})\right).

(226)

By convention we take
\prod_{l=k}^{k-1}\equiv 1
. Note that
J_{N}
is symmetric and so is
J_{N}^{-1}
. Therefore we have the approximation of each entry of the inverse.

Proof.

Applying the inverse formula for a tri-diagonal matrix (
90
), we have for
j\leq k

(J_{N}^{-1})_{j,k}=\frac{(-1)^{k-j}\gamma_{j}\beta_{k}}{\beta_{N}a_{N}\gamma_{N+1}}.

(227)

Choose any
a_{N}\neq 0
(e.g. pick
a_{N}=a_{N-1}
) and
Note that the inverse formula is independent on the choice of
a_{N}
. Recall that in the proof of Proposition
5.1
we have computed
\beta_{k}
to be (
139
), i.e., for
k=1,\dots,N

\beta_{k}=\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{1}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\omega_{l}^{+}}{\omega_{N-1}^{-}-\omega_{N-1}^{+}}\left(1+\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+C(k)\right)

(228)

where
C(k)
is defined in (
138
) with
|C(k)|\leq 4(1+c_{2})\varepsilon_{1}
and

\tilde{\beta}_{1}\coloneqq\omega_{N-1}^{-}a_{N-1}-b_{N-1}+z,\qquad\tilde{\beta}_{2}\coloneqq-\omega_{N-1}^{+}a_{N-1}+b_{N-1}-z.

(229)

Note that we have
\beta_{N}=a_{N-1},

\left|\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}\right|\leq c_{2}\varepsilon_{2}
.

Recall that transfer matrices
B_{j}
is defined as (
89
), and
\gamma_{j}
is defined recursively to be

\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{j}\\
\gamma_{j+1}\end{pmatrix}=B_{j}B_{j-1}\dots B_{2}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{1}\\
\gamma_{2}\end{pmatrix},\quad\gamma_{1}=a_{1},\quad\gamma_{2}=b_{0}-z.

(230)

Recall that the eigenvalues
\lambda_{j}^{+}
and
\lambda_{j}^{-}
of
B_{j}
are defined as (
87
).
B_{j}
can be diagonalised as
B_{j}=W_{j}\Lambda_{j}W_{j}^{-1}
, where
W_{j}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
\lambda_{j}^{+}&\lambda_{j}^{-}\end{pmatrix}
and
\Lambda_{j}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{j}^{+}&0\\
0&\lambda_{j}^{-}\end{pmatrix}
.

Further, define

D_{j}\coloneqq W_{j}^{-1}\left(B_{j}B_{j-1}\dots B_{2}-W_{j}\left(\prod_{l=2}^{j}\Lambda_{l}\right)W_{2}^{-1}\right)W_{2},\quad j=2,3,\dots,N,\quad\text{and }D_{1}\coloneqq 0.

(231)

We can rewrite (
230
) for all
j=2,3,\dots,N
to be

\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{j}\\
\gamma_{j+1}\end{pmatrix}=W_{j}\left(D_{j}+\prod_{l=2}^{j}\Lambda_{l}\right)W_{2}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{1}\\
\gamma_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

(232)

Let
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\coloneqq\lambda_{2}^{-}a_{1}-b_{0}+z
,
\tilde{\gamma}_{2}\coloneqq-\lambda_{2}^{+}a_{1}+b_{0}-z
such that
\frac{1}{\lambda_{2}^{-}-\lambda_{2}^{+}}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\\
\tilde{\gamma}_{2}\end{pmatrix}=W_{2}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{1}\\
\gamma_{2}\end{pmatrix}
. Then, we have for
j=2,\dots,N

\displaystyle\gamma_{j}=

\displaystyle\frac{\left((D_{j})_{1,1}+(D_{j})_{2,1}+\prod_{l=2}^{j}\lambda_{l}^{+}\right)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\left((D_{j})_{1,2}+(D_{j})_{2,2}+\prod_{l=2}^{j}\lambda_{l}^{-}\right)\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\lambda_{2}^{-}-\lambda_{2}^{+}}.

(233)

By convention, let
D_{1}\equiv 0
and
\prod_{l=2}^{1}\equiv 1
and we can allow
j=1,\dots,N
for the formula
\gamma_{j}
above.

For
\gamma_{N+1}
, we keep
B_{N}
unchanged, estimate from
B_{N-1}
and write

\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{N}\\
\gamma_{N+1}\end{pmatrix}=B_{N}W_{N-1}\left(D_{N-1}+\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\Lambda_{l}\right)W_{2}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{1}\\
\gamma_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

(234)

Note that

B_{N}W_{N-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{N-1}^{+}&\lambda_{N-1}^{-}\\
\frac{\left(b_{N-1}-z\right)\lambda_{N-1}^{+}-a_{N-1}}{a_{N}}&\frac{\left(b_{N-1}-z\right)\lambda_{N-1}^{-}-a_{N-1}}{a_{N}}\end{pmatrix}.

(235)

Denote

\tilde{\delta}_{1}\coloneqq\left(b_{N-1}-z\right)\lambda_{N-1}^{+}-a_{N-1},\qquad\tilde{\delta}_{2}\coloneqq\left(b_{N-1}-z\right)\lambda_{N-1}^{-}-a_{N-1}.

(236)

Compute the second entry of (
234
) to obtain

\gamma_{N+1}=\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{1}\left((D_{N-1})_{1,1}+\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}(D_{N-1})_{2,1}+\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\lambda_{l}^{+}\right)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\tilde{\delta}_{2}\left(\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}(D_{N-1})_{1,2}+(D_{N-1})_{2,2}+\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\lambda_{l}^{-}\right)\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{a_{N}\left(\lambda_{2}^{-}-\lambda_{2}^{+}\right)}.

(237)

By convention we let
D_{1}\equiv 0
and
\prod_{l=2}^{1}\equiv 1
and we can allow
j=1,\dots,N
for all above. Moreover, by the construction of
E_{j}
in (
92
), we have
E_{j}\equiv W_{j}^{-1}W_{j-1}-\mathrm{Id}
. Now we estimate
D_{j}
via the expansion

B_{j}B_{j-1}\dots B_{2}=W_{j}\Lambda_{j}(\mathrm{Id}+E_{j})\Lambda_{j-1}(\mathrm{Id}+E_{j-1})\dots\Lambda_{3}(\mathrm{Id}+E_{3})\Lambda_{2}W_{2}^{-1}.

(238)

Also, note that by assumption
b_{j}>0
, we have
|\lambda_{j}^{+}|>|\lambda_{j}^{-}|
. Hence,
\|W_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq|\lambda_{j}^{+}|
. Then, we have

\|D_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq\left(\prod_{l=2}^{j}\left(1+\|E_{l}\|_{\infty}\right)-1\right)\prod_{l=2}^{j}|\lambda_{l}^{+}|.

(239)

Now we are about to estimate
E_{j}
. We will do so by revealing some connections between
\lambda_{j}^{\pm}
and
\omega_{j}^{\pm}
and between
E_{j}
and
M_{j}
.

By (
218
), let
c_{01}=\frac{(1+\sqrt{5})c_{1}}{2c_{0}}
then

\displaystyle|\omega_{j}^{+}|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left|b_{j-1}-z\right|+\left(\left|b_{j-1}-z\right|^{2}+4|a_{j-1}a_{j}|\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2|a_{j-1}|}\leq c_{01},

(240)

\displaystyle|\omega_{j}^{-}|\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2|a_{j}|}{\left|b_{j-1}-z\right|+\left(\left|b_{j-1}-z\right|^{2}+4|a_{j-1}a_{j}|\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\geq\frac{1}{c_{01}}.

(241)

Note that each entry of the
2
by
2
matrix
E_{j}
is bounded by its operator norm.
Since
\lambda_{j}^{-}\omega_{j}^{+}=\lambda_{j}^{+}\omega_{j}^{-}=1
, by (
240
) and (
241
), we have each entry of
E_{j}
is bounded by
c_{01}^{2}\|M_{j}\|_{\infty}
. Hence,
\|E_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 3c_{01}^{2}\|M_{j}\|_{\infty}
. By (
220
) and
0<3c_{01}^{2}\varepsilon_{1}<1
, by the same engagement as in (
137
), we further estimate (
239
) to be

\|D_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 6c_{01}^{2}\varepsilon_{1}\prod_{l=2}^{j}|\lambda_{l}^{+}|.

(242)

Then, let

\displaystyle D(j)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left((D_{j})_{1,1}+(D_{j})_{2,1}\right)\prod_{l=2}^{j}(\lambda_{l}^{+})^{-1}+\left((D_{j})_{1,2}+(D_{j})_{2,2}\right)\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\prod_{l=2}^{j}(\lambda_{l}^{+})^{-1},

(243)

\displaystyle\tilde{D}(N-1)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left((D_{N-1})_{1,1}+\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}(D_{N-1})_{2,1}\right)\prod_{l=2}^{N}(\lambda_{l}^{+})^{-1}+\left((D_{N-1})_{1,2}+\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}(D_{N-1})_{2,2}\right)\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}(\lambda_{l}^{+})^{-1}.

(244)

Hence, by (
242
)

|D(j)|\leq 12\left(1+\left|\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\right|\right)c_{01}^{2}\varepsilon_{1},\quad|\tilde{D}(N-1)|\leq 6\left(1+\left|\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}\right|\right)\left(1+\left|\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\right|\right)c_{01}^{2}\varepsilon_{1}.

(245)

Note that
\lambda_{j}^{-}\omega_{j}^{+}=\lambda_{j}^{+}\omega_{j}^{-}=1
and we have
\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}=\frac{\left(b_{N-1}-z\right)\lambda_{N-1}^{-}-a_{N-1}}{\left(b_{N-1}-z\right)\lambda_{N-1}^{+}-a_{N-1}}=\frac{\omega_{N-1}^{-}}{\omega_{N-1}^{+}}\frac{\omega_{N-1}^{+}a_{N-1}-b_{N-1}+z}{\omega_{N-1}^{-}a_{N-1}-b_{N-1}+z}
and
\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}=\frac{-\lambda_{2}^{+}a_{1}+b_{0}-z}{\lambda_{2}^{-}a_{1}-b_{0}+z}=\frac{\omega_{2}^{+}}{\omega_{2}^{-}}\frac{-a_{1}+\omega_{2}^{-}(b_{0}-z)}{a_{1}-\omega_{2}^{+}(b_{0}-z)}
. Then by (
240
) (
241
), we estimate,

\left|\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}\right|\leq c_{2},\quad\left|\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\right|\leq c_{01}^{2}c_{2}.

(246)

Recall that
c_{2}\geq 1
. Further define

\tilde{\varepsilon_{1}}\coloneqq 6(1+c_{2})\left(1+c_{01}^{2}c_{2}^{2}\right)c_{01}^{2}\varepsilon_{1}.

(247)

Then by (
242
) we have
|C(k)|,|D(j)|,|\tilde{D}(N-1)|<\tilde{\varepsilon_{1}}
for all
j,k
. Also, note that by definition we have the following handy relations

\lambda_{j}^{-}=(\omega_{j}^{+})^{-1},\qquad\lambda_{j}^{+}=(\omega_{j}^{-})^{-1},\qquad\omega_{j}^{+}\omega_{j}^{-}=\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j-1}},\qquad\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}=-\omega_{N-1}^{-}.

(248)

Hence for
j\leq k

(J_{N}^{-1})_{j,k}=\frac{(-1)^{k-j}\beta_{k}}{\beta_{N}a_{N}}\frac{\gamma_{j}}{\gamma_{N+1}}\\
=\frac{(-1)^{k-j}\beta_{k}}{a_{N-1}a_{N}}\frac{\left((D_{j})_{1,1}+(D_{j})_{2,1}+\prod_{l=2}^{j}\lambda_{l}^{+}\right)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\left((D_{j})_{1,2}+(D_{j})_{2,2}+\prod_{l=2}^{j}\lambda_{l}^{-}\right)\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}\left((D_{N-1})_{1,1}+\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}(D_{N-1})_{2,1}+\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\lambda_{l}^{+}\right)\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\tilde{\delta}_{2}\left(\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}{\tilde{\delta}_{2}}(D_{N-1})_{1,2}+(D_{N-1})_{2,2}+\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\lambda_{l}^{-}\right)\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}\\
=\frac{(-1)^{k-j}\omega_{N-1}^{-}}{a_{k-1}}\frac{\prod_{l=j}^{k-1}\omega_{l}^{-}}{\left(\omega_{N-1}^{+}-\omega_{N-1}^{-}\right)\omega_{j}^{-}}\frac{\left(1+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\prod_{l=2}^{j}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+D(j)\right)\left(1+\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{2}}{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+C(k)\right)}{\left(1+\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+\tilde{D}(N-1)\right)}.

(249)

Let

\displaystyle(T_{N}(\eta))_{j,k}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{k-j}\omega_{N-1}^{-}}{a_{k-1}}\frac{\prod_{l=j}^{k-1}\omega_{l}^{-}}{\left(\omega_{N-1}^{+}-\omega_{N-1}^{-}\right)\omega_{j}^{-}}\frac{\left(1+D(j)\right)\left(1+C(k)\right)}{\left(1+\frac{\tilde{\delta}_{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\delta}_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+\tilde{D}(N-1)\right)},

(250)

\displaystyle H_{N}(\eta)\coloneqq

\displaystyle J_{N}^{-1}-T_{N}(\eta).

(251)

Hence as
\varepsilon_{1}\to 0
we have

\displaystyle\frac{(T_{N}(\eta))_{j,k}}{(T_{N}(\eta))_{j+l,k+l}}=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{m=j}^{k-1}\frac{\omega_{m}^{-}}{\omega_{m+l}^{-}}\right)\frac{\omega_{l+j}^{-}a_{k+l-1}}{\omega_{j}^{-}a_{k-1}}\frac{\left(1+D(j)\right)\left(1+C(k)\right)}{\left(1+D(j+l)\right)\left(1+C(k+l)\right)}

(252)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{m=j}^{k-1}\frac{\omega_{m}^{-}}{\omega_{m+l}^{-}}\right)\frac{\omega_{l+j}^{-}a_{k+l-1}}{\omega_{j}^{-}a_{k-1}}\left(1+O(\varepsilon_{1})\right).

(253)

Hence, whenever
\tilde{\varepsilon_{1}}+c_{01}^{2}c_{2}\varepsilon_{2}<1

\left|\frac{\omega_{N-1}^{-}\left(1+D(j)\right)\left(1+C(k)\right)}{\left(1+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}\prod_{l=2}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+\tilde{D}(N-1)\right)}\right|\leq\frac{c_{01}(1+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})^{2}}{1-c_{01}^{2}c_{2}\varepsilon_{2}-\tilde{\varepsilon_{1}}}\eqqcolon Constant^{\prime}.

(254)

Hence,

\displaystyle\left|(T_{N}(\eta))_{j,k}\right|\leq

\displaystyle Constant^{\prime}\left|\frac{\prod_{l=j}^{k-1}\omega_{l}^{-}}{a_{k-1}\omega_{j}^{-}\left(\omega_{N-1}^{-}-\omega_{N-1}^{+}\right)}\right|,

(255)

\displaystyle\left|(H_{N}(\eta))_{j,k}\right|\leq

\displaystyle 2Constant^{\prime}\left|\frac{\left(\prod_{l=j}^{k-1}\omega_{l}^{-}\right)\left(\prod_{l=2}^{j}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}+\prod_{l=k}^{N-1}\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}\right)}{a_{k-1}\left(\omega_{N-1}^{-}-\omega_{N-1}^{+}\right)}\right|.

(256)

Take the
Constant=2Constant^{\prime}
and we conclude the proof of Proposition
6.1
.
∎

6.2
Proof of Proposition
4.3

Proof of Proposition
4.3
.

Recall that we define
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
as (
94
), which can be regarded as a three-block diagonal matrix. The non-trivial block of
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
is where the entries have the indices both belonging to this set
I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
, defined as (
143
), .i.e., for all
j,k\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}

\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)_{j,k}=\left(P_{n+2mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-2mn^{\beta}}J^{(r)}Q_{n-2mn^{\beta}}P_{n+2mn^{\beta}}\right)_{j,k}.

(257)

In the following, we apply Proposition
6.1
to this non-trivial block of
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
, i.e., (
257
).
To shorten the notation, let

b_{j}=b_{j,n}-x_{0},\quad a_{j}=a_{j,n},\quad\text{and }z=\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}.

(258)

Then, at the left edge, for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
,
a_{j}
and
b_{j}
satisfy Conditions
5.1
and
5.2
. Hence, the quantities in (
218
) are bounded below and above as desired.

By Lemma
5.1
, we have
b_{l}>0
, for all
l\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}
. By Lemma
5.2
, we have as
n\to\infty

\max_{l\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}|\omega_{l}^{-}-1|=O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}),\quad\max_{l\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}|\omega_{l}^{+}-1|=O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})

(259)

Hence, as
n\to\infty

\left|\frac{-a_{n-2mn^{\beta}}+\omega_{n-2mn^{\beta}+1}^{-}(b_{n-2mn^{\beta}-1}-z)}{a_{n-2mn^{\beta}}-\omega_{n-2mn^{\beta}+1}^{+}(b_{n-2mn^{\beta}-1}-z)}\right|=1+O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}),\quad\left|\frac{-\omega_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}^{+}a_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}+b_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}-z}{\omega_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}^{-}a_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}-b_{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}+z}\right|=1+O(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}).

This shows
c_{2}=1+O(n^{-\alpha/2})
in (
219
).

Let
M_{j}
be defined as (
88
). By Lemma
5.3
we have as
n\to\infty

4mn^{\beta}\max_{l\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}\|M_{l}\|_{\infty}=o(n^{-2\beta+\alpha}).

(260)

This shows
\varepsilon_{1}=o(n^{-2\beta+\alpha})
in (
220
). Moreover, use Lemma
5.2
again to obtain that there exists a constant
d>0
such that for all
n>n_{0}

\prod_{l=n-2mn^{\beta}+2}^{n+2mn^{\beta}-1}\left|\frac{\omega_{l}^{-}}{\omega_{l}^{+}}\right|\leq\left(1-\frac{d}{n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right)^{4mn^{\beta}-2}\leq e^{-dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(4mn^{\beta}-2)}.

(261)

This shows
\varepsilon_{2}=O(e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}})
in (
128
), which is exponentially small.

Hence, the assumptions of Proposition
6.1
hold for the middle block of
J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}
. Hence, we obtain the estimate of
T_{n\pm 2mn\beta}(\eta_{r})
to be (
225
). Further, by
a_{j}=a_{n}+O(n^{\beta-1})
, (
225
) and Lemma
5.2
, we have as
n\to\infty

(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r}))_{j,k}=\frac{(-sgn(a_{N-1}))^{k-j}}{2a_{n}\left(\frac{-\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\prod_{l=j}^{k-1}\left(1-\left(\frac{-\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\xi_{l}\right)(1+o(1)),

(262)

where
\xi_{l}\coloneqq\omega_{l}^{-}sgn(a_{N-1})-\left(1-\left(\frac{-\eta}{n^{\alpha}|a_{N-1}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)=o\left(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)
. Note that this formula is for the left edge and for the right edges one should replace
a_{j}
by
-a_{j}
, as is described in Condition
5.2
.

To prove (
110
). Note that since each entry of
M_{l}
is bounded by the operator norm, we have for all
l=n-2mn^{\beta},\dots,n+2mn^{\beta}
,

|\omega_{l}^{-}-\omega_{l+1}^{-}|\leq\|M_{l}\|_{\infty}|\omega_{l}^{-}-\omega_{l}^{+}|.

By Lemma
5.3
we have
\|M_{l}\|_{\infty}=o(n^{-3\beta+\alpha})
, given that
|\omega_{l}^{-}-\omega_{l}^{+}|
is of order
n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}
. Hence we have

\max_{l\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}}|\omega_{l}^{-}-\omega_{l+1}^{-}|=o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}),\quad\text{ as }n\to\infty.

(263)

Lemma
5.2
says that
\omega_{m}^{-}
is bounded away from zero, hence we estimate (
226
) to be

\displaystyle\frac{(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r}))_{j,k}}{(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r}))_{j+l,k+l}}=

\displaystyle\left(1+o(n^{-3\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}})\right)^{(k-j)l}\left(1+O(n^{\beta-1})\right)\left(1+O(\varepsilon_{1})\right),\quad\text{ as }n\to\infty.

(264)

Note that
1+O(n^{\beta-1})=\frac{a_{k+l-1}}{a_{k-1}}
comes from Condition
5.1
. Also note that
\varepsilon_{1}=o(n^{-2\beta+\alpha})
. Moreover,
(k-j)l=O(n^{2\beta})
, since
1\leq k-j,l\leq 4mn^{\beta}
. Note that all the of the big-
O
s and small-
o
are uniform in the indices by assumptions. Together with the assumption
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}<1
, we have

\frac{(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r}))_{j,k}}{(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r}))_{j+l,k+l}}=1+o(n^{-\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}),\quad\text{ as }n\to\infty.

This shows the statement (
110
).

Note that above argument is taken at the left edge i.e.,
b_{j}>0
as assumed in (
149
). For the right edge, we have
b_{j}<0
. Then we consider
-J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
whose diagonals are
-b_{j}+\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}
and off diagonals are
-a_{j}
. Apply the result above by replacing
a_{n}
by
-a_{n}
and
\eta_{r}
by
-\eta_{r}
to estimate entries of
\left(-J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}
as in the statement. Then the result at the right edge is obtained by the simple fact
\left(J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}=-\left(-J_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}
.

This completes the proof.
∎

6.3
Proof of Proposition
4.4

Now we are ready to prove the Proposition
4.4
by Lemmas
5.4
,
5.5
and
5.6
and Proposition
4.3
.

Proof of Proposition
4.4
.

In the following we consider
\mathcal{A}=F_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
and
\mathcal{B}=\sum_{r}c_{r}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)
. Recall that the operator norm of a linear operator can be estimated by entries, i.e.,
\|\mathcal{B}\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}\sup_{k}|(\mathcal{B})_{k,k+j}|
. Together with (
109
) in Proposition
4.3
, we compute that
n^{-\alpha}\|\mathcal{B}\|_{\infty}
is uniformly bounded for all
n
. That is there exists a constant
C_{op}>0
such that
\|\mathcal{A}\|_{\infty},\|\mathcal{B}\|_{\infty}\leq n^{\alpha}C_{op}
.

By (
109
) in Proposition
4.3
, we have for all
|j-k|\geq n^{\beta}

\left|\left(\mathcal{B}\right)_{j,k}\right|\leq C_{0}n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(265)

Then use the same proof as in Lemma
5.4
, we have for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
with
l\leq m
,

\|P_{n}\mathcal{B}^{l}Q_{n+mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}\leq C_{m}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(266)

Hence, by Lemma
5.7
, we get

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{A})^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots(\mathcal{A})^{l_{j}}P_{n}-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{A}^{m}P_{n})-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{B})^{l_{1}}P_{n}\cdots(\mathcal{B})^{l_{j}}P_{n}+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathcal{B}^{m}P_{n})\right|\\
\leq(m+1)\left(\left(n^{\alpha}C_{op}\right)^{m-1}\|(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}+R_{n}\right).

(267)

Note that,

\|(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}\leq\sum_{r}|c_{r}|\|H_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1},

where
H_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})
is defined in (
251
). Thus by Proposition
4.3
, we have
