\|H_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})P_{n+mn^{\beta}}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j=n-2mn^{\beta}+1}^{n+2mn^{\beta}}\sum_{k=n-mn^{\beta}+1}^{n+mn^{\beta}}\\
Constant\quad n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\left(\left(1-\frac{d}{n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right)^{\max\{j,k\}-n+2mn^{\beta}}+\left(1-\frac{d}{n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right)^{n+2mn^{\beta}-\min\{j,k\}}\right)\\
\leq Constant\quad 16mn^{2\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}e^{-dmn^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(268)

This shows that (
267
) is exponentially small. Hence plug this estimate into the definition (
64
) and we have
\left|\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(\mathcal{A})-\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(\mathcal{B})\right|\leq C_{m}^{\prime\prime}e^{-d^{\prime\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}
.
Combining with Proposition
4.2
, we conclude (
112
).

∎

7
Proof of Proposition
4.5

Before we prove the Proposition
4.5
, we need to study the limiting behaviour of

\log\det\left(\mathrm{Id}+P_{n}\left(e^{tn^{-\alpha}\sum_{r}c_{r}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)}-\mathrm{Id}\right)P_{n}\right)e^{-t\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}n^{-\alpha}\sum_{r}c_{r}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)},

(269)

where
T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}
comes from Proposition
4.3
. In Subsection
7.1
, we will first give an abstract determinant estimate cf. Proposition
7.1
. Then apply it to prove Proposition
4.5
in Subsection
7.2
.

7.1
Limiting Behaviour of Fredholm Determinants

In the following we assume
N=N_{n}
is an even integer. Let
\vartheta=\vartheta_{n}>0
and
w=w_{n}>0
. Consider
A=A(n),B=B(n)\in\mathbb{C}^{N\times N}
whose operator norms are bounded by a uniform constant
M_{0}
. In this subsection we suppress the parameter
n
when this causes no confusion. Assume that
A
and
B
satisfy the following entry bound, with constants
C_{0},d_{0}>0
,

|A_{i,j}|+|B_{i,j}|\leq\frac{C_{0}}{w}e^{-d_{0}|i-j|/w}.

(270)

|A_{i+h,j+h}-A_{i,j}|+|B_{i+h,j+h}-B_{i,j}|\leq\frac{\vartheta}{w}e^{-d_{0}|i-j|/w},

(271)

whenever all indices belong to
\{1,\dots,N\}
. These assumptions mean that
A
is close enough to a Toeplitz matrix with a small error. Namely, define
T_{A}
to be a Toeplitz matrix of size
N
with symbol
u^{A}
such that

u^{A}_{i}\coloneqq\begin{cases}A_{i+1,1},\quad i\geq 0\\
A_{1,1-i},\quad i<0\end{cases},\qquad(T_{A})_{i,j}\coloneqq u^{A}_{i-j}.

(272)

A direct consequence of (
270
) is that
|u_{i}^{A}|\leq\frac{C_{0}}{w}e^{-d_{0}|i|/w}
. Define
T_{B}
and
u^{B}_{i}
analogously.

Lemma 7.1
.

Fix
0<b<d_{0}
and define a matrix norm
\|\cdot\|_{\infty,b}
such that

\|X\|_{\infty,b}=\max\left\{\sup_{i}\sum_{j}|X_{i,j}|e^{b|i-j|/w},\sup_{j}\sum_{i}|X_{i,j}|e^{b|i-j|/w}\right\}.

(273)

The norm is well-defined and submultiplicative, and
\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\|X\|_{\infty,b}
.

Proof.

The triangle inequality
|i-j|\leq|i-k|+|k-j|
proves
submultiplicativity. Domination of the operator norm comes from the operator norm bound
\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\sup_{i}\sum_{j}|X_{i,j|}
.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Assume
A
and
B
satisfy (
270
) and (
271
). Then there exists a universal constant
c>0
such that

\left\|[A,B]-[T_{A},T_{B}]\right\|_{1}\leq c\frac{\vartheta N}{w}.

(274)

Moreover, define four Hankel matrices
(H_{\pm,A})_{i,j}=u^{A}_{\pm(i+j-1)}
and
(H_{\pm,B})_{i,j}=u^{B}_{\pm(i+j-1)}
. Then

[A,B]=-H_{+,A}H_{-,B}+H_{+,B}H_{-,A}+W(-H_{-,A}H_{+,B}+H_{-,B}H_{+,A})W+E,

(275)

where
W
is the reflection operator such that
We_{j}=e_{N+1-j}
and the remainder
E
is bounded as (
274
).

Proof.

Define
D_{A}\coloneqq A-T_{A}
and
D_{B}\coloneqq B-T_{B}
. Then by (
271
) we have
\|D_{A}\|_{\infty}+\|D_{B}\|_{\infty}\leq c_{D}\vartheta
, for some universal constant
c_{D}>0
only depending on
d_{0}
.
Use the definition of commutator to obtain

[A,B]-[T_{A},T_{B}]=[D_{A},B]+[T_{A},D_{B}].

(276)

Then use entry-wise estimate and (
270
) we have there exists a universal constant
c_{1}>0
such that

\|B\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}|B_{i,j}|^{2}}\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\frac{N}{w}}.

(277)

Similarly, we also have
\|T_{A}\|_{2}\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\frac{N}{w}}
. For
D_{A}
, we use (
271
) to obtain that

\|D_{A}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}|A_{i,j}-{T_{A}}_{i,j}|^{2}}\leq c\vartheta\sqrt{\frac{N}{w}}.

(278)

Similarly, we have
\|D_{B}\|_{2}\leq c\vartheta\sqrt{\frac{N}{w}}
. Hence, using
\|XY\|_{1}\leq\|X\|_{2}\|Y\|_{2}
, we obtain

\|[D_{A},B]\|_{1}\leq 2\|D_{A}\|_{2}\|B\|_{2}\leq 2c_{1}^{2}\frac{\vartheta N}{w}.

(279)

Similarly, we have
\|[T_{A},D_{B}]\|_{1}\leq c\frac{\vartheta N}{w}
. Then plug these estimates into (
276
) to conclude (
274
). Apply the finite Toeplitz and Hankel identity to
[T_{A},T_{B}]
to conclude (
275
), where we take
E=[A,B]-[T_{A},T_{B}]
.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume
A
and
B
satisfy (
270
) and (
271
). For any matrices
X
and
Y
, we have

\|P_{N/2}X[A,B]YQ_{N/2}\|_{1}+\|Q_{N/2}X[A,B]YP_{N/2}\|_{1}\leq\|X\|_{\infty,b}\|Y\|_{\infty,b}\left(\frac{c\vartheta N}{w}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{b^{2}}e^{-bN/(2w)}\right),

(280)

where
P_{N/2}
is the projection onto the first
N/2
coordinates and
Q_{N/2}=\mathrm{Id}-P_{N/2}
.

Proof.

Let
H
be any of the Hankel matrices in
(
275
), then by definition we have
|(H)_{k,j}|\leq\frac{C_{0}}{w}e^{-d_{0}(k+j-1)/w}
.
Since
i+j-1\leq|i-k|+k+j-1
and
b<d_{0}
, then we have

|(XH)_{ij}|\leq\frac{C_{0}}{w}\sum_{k}|X_{ik}|e^{-b(k+j-1)/w}\leq\frac{C_{0}\|X\|_{\infty,b}}{w}e^{-b(i+j-1)/w}.

Summing squares, first over all entries and then over the rows or columns
beyond
N/2
, gives

\|XH\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{0}\|X\|_{\infty,b}}{2b},\quad\|XHQ_{N/2}\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{0}\|X\|_{\infty,b}}{2b}e^{-bN/(2w)}.

(281)

Consequently, for
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
to be any of the Hankel matrices in
(
275
), we have

\|P_{N/2}XH_{1}H_{2}YQ_{N/2}\|_{1}\leq\|P_{N/2}XH_{1}\|_{2}\|H_{2}YQ_{N/2}\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{0}^{2}\|X\|_{\infty,b}\|Y\|_{\infty,b}}{4b^{2}}e^{-bN/(2w)}.

(282)

Similarly, we obtain the same bound for
\|Q_{N/2}XH_{1}H_{2}YP_{N/2}\|_{1}
.

For the conjugation
W
, we have

\|WXW\|_{\infty,b}=\|X\|_{\infty,b}
,
WP_{N/2}W=Q_{N/2}
and
W^{2}=\mathrm{Id}
. Hence,

\|P_{N/2}XWH_{1}H_{2}WYQ_{N/2}\|_{1}\leq\|Q_{N/2}WXWH_{1}\|_{2}\|H_{2}WYWP_{N/2}\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{0}^{2}\|X\|_{\infty,b}\|Y\|_{\infty,b}}{4b^{2}}e^{-bN/(2w)}.

(283)

Finally,
\|XEY\|_{1}\leq\|X\|_{\infty,b}\|Y\|_{\infty,b}\frac{c\vartheta N}{w}
by Lemma
7.2
.
Adding these estimates concludes the lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Assume
A
and
B
satisfy (
270
) and (
271
).
Let

R(t;n)\coloneqq e^{-tA}e^{t(A+B)}e^{-tB},\quad H(t;n)\coloneqq R^{\prime}(t;n)R(t;n)^{-1},\quad\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}\coloneqq\frac{c\vartheta N}{w}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{b^{2}}e^{-bN/(2w)}.

(284)

Then uniformly for all
|t|<t_{0}

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}H(t;n)P_{N/2}+t\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}[A,B]P_{N/2}\right|\leq|t|^{2}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N},

(285)

for some universal constant
m_{t_{0},M_{0}}>0
only depends on
t_{0},M_{0},C_{0},d_{0},b
. Moreover,

\|P_{N/2}R(t;n)Q_{N/2}\|_{1}+\|Q_{N/2}R(t;n)P_{N/2}\|_{1}\leq|t|^{2}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N},\quad\|P_{N/2}H(t;n)Q_{N/2}\|_{1}\leq|t|m_{t_{0},M_{0}}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}.

(286)

Proof.

Direct differentiation gives
R^{\prime}=e^{-tA}[B,e^{t(A+B)}]e^{-tB}
.
Since
[B,A+B]=[B,A]
, by Duhamel’s identity

R^{\prime}(t)=t\int_{0}^{1}e^{-tA}e^{ut(A+B)}[B,A]e^{t(1-u)(A+B)}e^{-tB}\,du.

(287)

Multiplication by
R(t)^{-1}
gives

H(t)=t\int_{0}^{1}J_{u}(t)\,du,\quad\text{ where }J_{u}(s)\coloneqq V_{u}(s)[B,A]V_{u}(s)^{-1},\quad V_{u}(s)\coloneqq e^{-sA}e^{us(A+B)}.

(288)

By Lemma
7.1

\max\{\|V_{u}(s)\|_{\infty,b},\|V_{u}(s)^{-1}\|_{\infty,b}\}\leq e^{t_{0}(\|A\|_{\infty,b}+\|B\|_{\infty,b})}\eqqcolon C_{t_{0},M_{0}}
. Define

M_{u}(s)\coloneqq V_{u}^{\prime}(s)V_{u}(s)^{-1}=-A+ue^{-sA}(A+B)e^{sA}.

(289)

Then by direct computation we have
M_{u}(s)=-V_{u}(s)(V_{u}(s)^{-1})^{\prime}
. Hence

J_{u}^{\prime}(s)=[M_{u}(s),J_{u}(s)].

(290)

Insert
P_{N/2}+Q_{N/2}=\mathrm{Id}
and take the trace to obtain

\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{N/2}[M_{u}(s),J_{u}(s)]P_{N/2})=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{N/2}M_{u}(s)Q_{N/2}J_{u}(s)P_{N/2}-P_{N/2}J_{u}(s)Q_{N/2}M_{u}(s)P_{N/2}).

(291)

Note that
\|M_{u}(s)\|_{\infty}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}+e^{2t_{0}\|A\|_{\infty}}\|A+B\|_{\infty}<c_{t_{0},M_{0}}
for some universal constant
c_{t_{0},M_{0}}
. Lemma
7.3
, with

X=V_{u}(s)
and
Y=V_{u}(s)^{-1}
, now gives

\left|\frac{d}{ds}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{N/2}J_{u}(s)P_{N/2})\right|\leq\|M_{u}(s)\|_{\infty}\bigl(\|Q_{N/2}J_{u}(s)P_{N/2}\|_{1}+\|P_{N/2}J_{u}(s)Q_{N/2}\|_{1}\bigr)\leq c_{t_{0},M_{0}}C_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{2}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}.

(292)

Since
J_{u}(0)=[B,A]
, integration from zero to
t
implies

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}H(t)P_{N/2}-t\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}[B,A]P_{N/2}\right|=\left|t\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}J_{u}^{\prime}(s)P_{N/2}dsdu\right|\leq c_{t_{0},M_{0}}C_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{2}|t|^{2}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N},

(293)

which proves (
285
).

For the rest of the statement, note that
R(0)=\mathrm{Id}
. Hence similarly by (
287
) and Lemma
7.3
we have

\|P_{N/2}R(t)Q_{N/2}\|_{1}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|P_{N/2}R^{\prime}(s)Q_{N/2}\|_{1}ds\leq|t|^{2}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N},

(294)

for some uniform constant
m_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{\prime}>0
. Similarly we have the same upper bound for
\|Q_{N/2}R(t)P_{N/2}\|_{1}
. By (
288
) and Lemma
7.3
,
\|P_{N/2}H(t)Q_{N/2}\|_{1}\leq|t|m_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.1
.

Fix
t_{0}>0
.
Suppose that
A,B\in\mathbb{C}^{N\times N}

satisfy (
270
) and
(
271
). Then there exist constants
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
C_{t_{0},m_{0}}>0

depending only on
t_{0},M_{0},C_{0},d_{0}
and
b
, such that, whenever
\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}\leq\varepsilon_{0}

the matrix

F(t)\coloneqq Q_{N/2}+P_{N/2}e^{-tA}e^{t(A+B)}e^{-tB}P_{N/2}

(295)

is invertible for every
|t|\leq t_{0}
, and

\left|\log\det F(t)+\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}[A,B]P_{N/2}\right|\leq C_{t_{0},m_{0}}(|t|^{3}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}+|t|^{4}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}^{2}),

(296)

Proof.

Set
R(t)
and
H(t)
as in Lemma
7.4
. Note that

F(t)\left(Q_{N/2}+P_{N/2}R(t)^{-1}P_{N/2}\right)=\mathrm{Id}-P_{N/2}R(t)Q_{N/2}R(t)^{-1}P_{N/2}.

(297)

Then by (
286
) in Lemma
7.4
we can choose
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, depending only on

t_{0},M_{0},C_{0},d_{0}
and
b
, sufficiently small that
C_{t_{0}}\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1/2
.
Then for all
|t|<t_{0}
, we have the matrix
F(t)
is invertible and

\|F(t)^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\|R(t)^{-1}\|_{\infty}}{1-\|R(t)\|_{\infty}|t|^{2}m_{t_{0}}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}}\leq c_{t_{0}}.

(298)

Use the derivative of log determinant

\frac{d}{dt}\log\det F(t)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}F^{\prime}(t)F(t)^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}H(t)P_{N/2}+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}H(t)Q_{N/2}R(t)P_{N/2}F(t)^{-1}

(299)

where the second equality uses the definition of
F(t)
and
H(t)
. Then by (
286
) in Lemma
7.4
we have

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}H(t)Q_{N/2}R(t)P_{N/2}F(t)^{-1}\right|\leq|t|^{3}c_{t_{0}}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{2}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}^{2}.

(300)

Together with (
285
) in Lemma
7.4
we have

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\log\det F(t)+t\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}[A,B]P_{N/2}\right|\leq|t|^{2}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}+|t|^{3}c_{t_{0}}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{2}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}^{2}.

(301)

Then integrating above from
0
to
t
implies

\left|log\det F(t)+\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}[A,B]P_{N/2}\right|\leq|t|^{3}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}+|t|^{4}c_{t_{0}}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{2}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}^{2}.

(302)

Then we can define the universal constant
C_{t_{0},M_{0}}=\max\{m_{t_{0},M_{0}},c_{t_{0}}m_{t_{0},M_{0}}^{2}\}
to conclude the Proposition.
∎

Corollary 7.1
.

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}[A,B]P_{N/2}-\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}k(u_{k}^{B}u_{-k}^{A}-u_{k}^{A}u_{-k}^{B})\right|\leq c\frac{\vartheta N}{w}+\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{d_{0}^{2}}e^{-d_{0}N/w}.

(303)

Proof.

By Lemma
7.2
, it is sufficient to compute

\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}[T_{A},T_{B}]P_{N/2}=\sum_{i=1}^{N/2}\sum_{j=N/2+1}^{N}u_{i-j}^{A}u_{j-i}^{B}-u_{i-j}^{B}u_{j-i}^{A}.

(304)

Then, taking
k=j-i
and relabelling the sum above, we get

\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{N/2}[T_{A},T_{B}]P_{N/2}=\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}k\left(u_{-k}^{A}u_{k}^{B}-u_{-k}^{B}u_{k}^{A}\right)+\sum_{k=N/2+1}^{N-1}(N-2k)\left(u_{-k}^{A}u_{k}^{B}-u_{-k}^{B}u_{k}^{A}\right).

(305)

Then by assumption (
270
) we can bound the second sum on the right-hand side of (
305
) to be

\sum_{k=N/2+1}^{N-1}2|N-2k|\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{w^{2}}e^{-2d_{0}k/w}=\sum_{j=1}^{N/2-1}4j\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{w^{2}}e^{-2d_{0}(N/2+j)/w}\leq\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{d_{0}^{2}}e^{-d_{0}N/w},

(306)

where for the inequality above we use the estimate
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{j}{x^{2}}e^{-2d_{0}j/x}=\frac{d}{dx}\frac{1}{2d_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}e^{-2d_{0}j/x}\leq\frac{1}{4d_{0}^{2}}
.
∎

In
[
9
]
, a generalisation of
the Helton–Howe–Pincus formula is used to obtain the central limit theorem for the mesoscopic bulk case. This generalisation was first proved by Ehrhardt
[
19
]
. That is for bounded operators
A,B
such that the commutator
[A,B]
is trace class, then

\det\left(e^{-A}e^{A+B}e^{-B}\right)=e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}[A,B]}.

(307)

However, for finite dimensional matrices
A
and
B
, the formula (
307
) is equivalent the constant
1
. Indeed, Proposition
7.1
reveals that the boundary terms caused by the projection
P_{N/2}
result in a non-trivial limit as
\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}\to 0
.

7.2
Proof of Proposition
4.5

Fix
m\geq 2
and define

T\coloneqq\sum_{r=1}^{2M}c_{r}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})=T_{-}+T_{+},

(308)

where
T_{-}
is strictly upper triangular and
T_{+}
is lower triangular.

Corollary 7.2
.

As
n\to\infty
uniformly for all
t
in a small disc, we have

e^{-tn^{-\alpha}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{n}TP_{n})}\det\left(\mathrm{Id}+P_{n}(e^{tn^{-\alpha}T}-\mathrm{Id})P_{n}\right)=\exp{\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2n^{2\alpha}}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}[T_{+},T_{-}]P_{n}+|t|^{3}o(1)\right)}.

(309)

Proof.

Since
T_{+}
is lower triangular, we have
P_{n}e^{T_{+}}P_{n}=P_{n}e^{T_{+}}
and similarly,
P_{n}e^{T_{-}}P_{n}=e^{T_{-}}P_{n}
. Then

e^{-tn^{-\alpha}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{n}TP_{n})}\det\left(\mathrm{Id}+P_{n}(e^{tn^{-\alpha}T}-\mathrm{Id})P_{n}\right)=\det\left(\mathrm{Id}+P_{n}(e^{-tn^{-\alpha}T_{+}}e^{tn^{-\alpha}T}e^{-tn^{-\alpha}T_{-}}-\mathrm{Id})P_{n}\right).

(310)

The entry estimates of Proposition
4.3
give

n^{-\alpha}|T_{j,k}|\leq Cn^{-\alpha/2}e^{-d_{0}|j-k|/n^{\alpha/2}},\quad n^{-\alpha}|T_{j,k}-T_{j+h,k+h}|\leq\frac{\vartheta}{n^{\alpha/2}}e^{-d_{0}|j-k|/n^{\alpha/2}},

where
\delta=o(n^{-\beta+\alpha/2})
, uniformly for the indices in the consideration. To apply Proposition
7.1
, we need to reorganise the coordinates. Restrict to
\{n-2mn^{\beta}+1,\ldots,n+2mn^{\beta}\}
and re-index from
1
to
N
. Then apply Proposition
7.1
with

N=4mn^{\beta},\qquad w=n^{\alpha/2},\qquad A=n^{-\alpha}T_{+},\qquad B=n^{-\alpha}T_{-},

(311)

and with
\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N}=o(1)
. Then we have (
310
) is estimated to be as
n\to\infty
uniformly for
t
in a small disc,

\exp{\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2n^{2\alpha}}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}[T_{-},T_{+}]P_{n}+O(|t|^{3}\varepsilon_{w,\vartheta,N})\right)}.

(312)

∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Consider the test function
f(x)=\sum_{r=1}^{2M}\frac{c_{r}}{x-\eta_{r}}
with
c_{j},\eta_{r}
defined as (
43
). Then we have

-\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2\alpha}}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}[T_{+},T_{-}]P_{n}=\sigma_{f}^{2}\equiv\begin{cases}\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(x^{2})-f(y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy&\quad\text{left edge,}\\
\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(-x^{2})-f(-y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy&\quad\text{right edge.}\end{cases}

(313)

Proof.

Let
N_{0}=n-2mn^{\beta}+1
. By Corollary
7.1
we have

-\frac{1}{n^{2\alpha}}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}[T_{+},T_{-}]P_{n}=\frac{1}{n^{2\alpha}}\sum_{r,s=1}^{2M}c_{r}c_{s}\sum_{k=1}^{4mn^{\beta}-1}k\left(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{r})\right)_{k+N_{0},N_{0}}\left(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}(\eta_{s})\right)_{N_{0},k+N_{0}}+o(1).

(314)

We estimate each entry of the three-fold sum (
314
), by Proposition
4.3
, that is for any
r,s
and
j,l

\left(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)\right)_{j,l}\left(T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{s}\right)\right)_{l,j}=\frac{n^{\alpha}(1+o(1))}{4a_{n,n}^{2}\left(\frac{-\eta_{r}}{|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{-\eta_{s}}{|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\prod\limits_{i=j}^{l-1}\left(1-\left(-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(-\frac{\eta_{s}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\xi_{i}^{(r,s)}\right),

(315)

as
n\to\infty
, where
\xi_{i}^{(r,s)}\coloneqq\xi_{i}^{(r)}+\xi_{i}^{(s)}+\xi_{i}^{(r)}\xi_{i}^{(s)}-\xi_{i}^{(r)}\left(-\frac{\eta_{s}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\xi_{i}^{(s)}\left(-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|a_{n,n}|n^{\alpha}}=o(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})
.

Note that for all
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta
we have
\left(1-zn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)^{n^{\beta}}=O(e^{-\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}zn^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}})
for any
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z>0
, which is exponentially small. Hence, adding exponentially small terms does not change the sum in the limit, i.e., as
n\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{n^{2\alpha}}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}[T_{+},T_{-}]P_{n}=\frac{(1+o(1))}{4n^{\alpha}a_{n,n}^{2}\left(\frac{-\eta_{r}}{|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{-\eta_{s}}{|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sum_{r,s=1}^{2M}c_{r}c_{s}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k\left(1-\left(-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(-\frac{\eta_{s}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{k}+o(1)\\
=\sum_{r,s=1}^{2M}c_{r}c_{s}\frac{1}{4\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}}+o(1).

(316)

The rest is to use the residue theorem to recover the double integral formula. By definition of
f
as (
43
)

\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(x^{2})-f(y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy=\sum_{r,s}c_{r}c_{s}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{x^{2}-\eta_{r}}-\frac{1}{y^{2}-\eta_{r}}}{x-y}\right)\left(\frac{\frac{1}{x^{2}-\eta_{s}}-\frac{1}{y^{2}-\eta_{s}}}{x-y}\right)dxdy.

Each of the double integrals can then be rewritten in the following way

\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{x^{2}-\eta_{r}}-\frac{1}{y^{2}-\eta_{r}}}{x-y}\right)\left(\frac{\frac{1}{x^{2}-\eta_{s}}-\frac{1}{y^{2}-\eta_{s}}}{x-y}\right)dxdy\\
=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{x^{2}}{(x^{2}-\eta_{r})(x^{2}-\eta_{s})}dx\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{(y^{2}-\eta_{r})(y^{2}-\eta_{s})}dy+2\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{x}{(x^{2}-\eta_{r})(x^{2}-\eta_{s})}dx\right)^{2}.

Apply the residue theorem to the three integrals above. On the principal square root branch, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(i\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}})>0
. So each integral has two residues to consider i.e.
i\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
i\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{x^{2}}{(x^{2}-\eta_{r})(x^{2}-\eta_{s})}dx=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2i(\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}})},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{(y^{2}-\eta_{r})(y^{2}-\eta_{s})}dy=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2i\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}})},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{x}{(x^{2}-\eta_{r})(x^{2}-\eta_{s})}dx=

\displaystyle 0.

Consequently,

\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(x^{2})-f(y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy=\sum_{r,s=1}^{2M}c_{r}c_{s}\frac{-1}{2\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(-\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(-\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}}.

Combining (
316
) and Lemma
5.2
, we conclude that, at the left edge,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2\alpha}}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}[T_{+},T_{-}]P_{n}=-\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(x^{2})-f(y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy.

(317)

Note that at the right edge instead of (
161
) we have

\displaystyle\omega_{l}^{(r)-}=\frac{x_{0}-b_{l,n}+\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}-\left(\left(x_{0}-b_{l,n}+\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{2}-4a_{l-1,n}a_{l,n}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{-2a_{l-1,n}}.

Then by the same proof of Lemma
5.2
we get
as
n\to\infty
, for
l\in\{n-2mn^{\beta},n-2mn^{\beta}+1,\dots,n+2mn^{\beta}\}
,

\displaystyle\omega_{l}^{(r)-}=\left(1-\left(\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}|a_{n,n}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)sgn(-a_{n,n})+o(n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}).

(318)

We can now follow the same computation and obtain that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2\alpha}}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}[T_{+},T_{-}]P_{n}=\sum_{r,s=1}^{2M}c_{r}c_{s}\frac{1}{4\left(\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}}.

(319)

From here the rest is to use the residue theorem to obtain the double integral formula. Recall the formulation of
f
and we have

\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(-x^{2})-f(-y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy=\sum_{r,s}c_{r}c_{s}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{x^{2}+\eta_{r}}-\frac{1}{y^{2}+\eta_{r}}}{x-y}\right)\left(\frac{\frac{1}{x^{2}+\eta_{s}}-\frac{1}{y^{2}+\eta_{s}}}{x-y}\right)dxdy.

Similar to the computation of the left edge, we rewrite each of the double integrals into multiplications of single integrals with poles at
i\left(\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
i\left(\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, on the principal square root branch. Apply the residue theorem and we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(-x^{2})-f(-y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy=\sum_{r,s=1}^{2M}c_{r}c_{s}\frac{-1}{2\left(\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\eta_{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\eta_{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}}.

Combining (
316
) and Lemma
5.2
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2\alpha}}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}[T_{+},T_{-}]P_{n}=-\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{f(-x^{2})-f(-y^{2})}{x-y}\right)^{2}dxdy.

(320)

∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
4.5
.

Proof of Proposition
4.5
.

Recall that we consider
f
defined as (
43
) and
F\coloneqq\sum_{r=1}^{2M}c_{r}\left(\mathcal{J}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}
.

Proposition
4.2
shows that the first
m
coefficients in the expansion around small
t
of below

\log\mathbb{E}[\exp\left(tX_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}-t\mathbb{E}[X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}]\right)]=\log\left(\det\left(\mathrm{Id}+P_{n}\left(e^{tn^{-\alpha}F}-\mathrm{Id}\right)P_{n}\right)e^{-tn^{-\alpha}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(P_{n}F\right)}\right)

(321)

are asymptotically the same as those of the following

\log\det\left(\mathrm{Id}+P_{n}\left(e^{tn^{-\alpha}\sum_{r}c_{r}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)}-\mathrm{Id}\right)P_{n}\right)e^{-tn^{-\alpha}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}P_{n}\sum_{r}c_{r}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)}\\
=\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{t^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{(n)}\left(\sum_{r}c_{r}n^{-\alpha}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)\right).

(322)

By Corollary
7.2
and Lemma
7.5
, we see that, as
n\to\infty

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{(n)}\left(\sum_{r}c_{r}n^{-\alpha}T_{n\pm 2mn^{\beta}}\left(\eta_{r}\right)\right)=\begin{cases}\sigma_{f}^{2},\quad&k=2\\
0,\quad&k=3,\dots m.\end{cases}

(323)

The consequence of Lemma
3.4
implies that the expansions of both of the right-hand sides of (
321
) and (
322
) are absolutely convergent. We conclude that, via the dominated convergence theorem,
for any
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}

around the edges, i.e.
x_{0}=b_{n-1,n}\pm 2\sqrt{a_{n,n}a_{n-1,n}}+o(n^{-\alpha})

\lim_{n\to\infty}\log\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(tX_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}-t\mathbb{E}[X_{f,\alpha,x_{0}}^{(n)}]\right)\right]=\frac{t^{2}\sigma_{f}^{2}}{2}.

(324)

Then by the moments method,
X_{n}(f_{\alpha,x_{0}})-\mathbb{E}[X_{n}(f_{\alpha,x_{0}})]
has the Gaussian fluctuations in the limit.
∎

Hence, together with Subsection
4.2
and we complete the proof of Theorem
2.3
.

8
Proof of Theorems
1.2
,
1.1
,
2.2

We will use Theorem
2.3
to prove Theorems
1.1
and
2.2
. We will prove Theorem
1.2
by Theorem
2.2
.

Proof of Theorem
2.2
.

For
0<\alpha<\frac{2}{3}
, condition (
2.1
) implies that for all
j\in I^{(\alpha,\varepsilon)}_{n}
, as
n\to\infty
,

a_{j,n}a_{j-2,n}-a_{j-1,n}^{2}=O(n^{-1}),\quad(b_{j-1,n}-x_{0}-a_{j,n})a_{j-2,n}-(b_{j-2,n}-x_{0}-a_{j-1,n})a_{j-1,n}=O(n^{-1}).

Hence the conditions in Theorem
2.3
are satisfied. Then apply Theorem
2.3
and we conclude the proof of Theorem
2.2
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

The asymptotics of recurrence coefficients of the modified Jacobi polynomials with measure (
3
) are given by the Riemann-Hilbert method in
[
28
]
to be

a_{j}=\frac{1}{2}-\left(\frac{4\gamma_{1}^{2}-1}{32}+\frac{4\gamma_{2}^{2}-1}{32}\right)\frac{1}{j^{2}}+O(j^{-3}),\quad b_{j}=\frac{\gamma_{2}^{2}-\gamma_{1}^{2}}{4j^{2}}+O(j^{-3}),\quad\text{as }j\to\infty,

(325)

where
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
are some constants explicitly given in
[
28
]
.
Note that there is no scaling in this model and, hence, we remove the second subscript
n
in the notations. Hence,

\displaystyle|a_{j}-a_{j-1}|=O(j^{-3}),\quad|b_{j}-b_{j-1}|=O(j^{-3}),\qquad\text{as }j\to\infty.

Then the conditions (
28
) and (
29
) are satisfied for all
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
.Hence the limit of mesoscopic fluctuations holds for all
\alpha\in(0,2)
. Then apply Theorem
2.3
, take
\beta\to 1
and we conclude the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Let
j\in I_{n}^{(\alpha,\varepsilon)}
, which is defined in (
21
). Let
c=\frac{j}{n}
. Then we rewrite

\displaystyle e^{-nV(x)}dx=e^{-\frac{j}{c}V(x)}dx.

(326)

Then the conditions on
V
and positivity about
c
imply the existence and uniqueness of the equilibrium measure of
c^{-1}V
, denoted by
\mu_{c^{-1}V}
by the potential theory. By the convexity of
c^{-1}V
, the support of
\mu_{c^{-1}V}
is a single interval say
[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}]
, which is determined by the following set of equations,

\displaystyle\int_{\alpha_{1}}^{\alpha_{2}}\frac{V^{\prime}(s)}{\sqrt{(s-\alpha_{1})(\alpha_{2}-s)}}ds=0,\quad\int_{\alpha_{1}}^{\alpha_{2}}\frac{sV^{\prime}(s)}{\sqrt{(s-\alpha_{1})(\alpha_{2}-s)}}ds=2\pi c.

(327)

In the literature
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
are called the Mhaskar-Rakhmanov-Saff numbers, (cf.
[
43
]
pages 203-234)
Let
F:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
be a real analytic function such that
F(x,y,c)\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\phi(x,y)\\
\varphi(x,y,c)\end{pmatrix}
where

\displaystyle\phi(x,y)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}V^{\prime}\left(\frac{(y-x)t+x+y}{2}\right)\frac{dt}{\sqrt{1-t^{2}}},

\displaystyle\varphi(x,y,c)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{(y-x)t}{2}V^{\prime}\left(\frac{(y-x)t+x+y}{2}\right)\frac{dt}{\sqrt{1-t^{2}}}-2\pi c.

Then we have
F(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},c)=\mathbf{0}
by (
327
) and a change of variable argument. We further define

\displaystyle d\mathcal{F}(t)\coloneqq V^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{(\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1})t+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}{2}\right)\frac{dt}{\sqrt{1-t^{2}}}.

Then by strict convexity of
V
, we have
V^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
and does not vanish on any non-empty open interval. Thus
\mathcal{F}(t)
defines a positive measure. Now computing the Jacobian of
F
at
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},c)
, we get

D_{(x,y)}F(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},c)=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\left(1-t\right)d\mathcal{F}(t),&\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\left(1+t\right)d\mathcal{F}(t)\\
-\frac{2\pi c}{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}}+\frac{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}}{4}\int_{-1}^{1}t\left(1-t\right)d\mathcal{F}(t),&\frac{2\pi c}{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}}+\frac{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}}{4}\int_{-1}^{1}t\left(1+t\right)d\mathcal{F}(t)\end{pmatrix}.

(328)

The determinant of (
328
) can be computed as

\frac{4\pi c}{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}}\int_{-1}^{1}d\mathcal{F}(t)\\
+\left(\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}\right)\left(\int_{-1}^{1}\left(1-t\right)d\mathcal{F}(t)\int_{-1}^{1}t\left(1+t\right)d\mathcal{F}(t)-\int_{-1}^{1}\left(1+t\right)d\mathcal{F}(t)\int_{-1}^{1}t\left(1-t\right)d\mathcal{F}(t)\right)\\
=\frac{4\pi c}{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}}\int_{-1}^{1}d\mathcal{F}(t)+2\left(\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}\right)\left(\int_{-1}^{1}d\mathcal{F}(t)\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}d\mathcal{F}(t)-\left(\int_{-1}^{1}td\mathcal{F}(t)\right)^{2}\right).

Use Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and we deduce
\left(\int_{-1}^{1}td\mathcal{F}(t)\right)^{2}\leq\int_{-1}^{1}t^{2}d\mathcal{F}(t)\int_{-1}^{1}d\mathcal{F}(t)
. Subsequently, the Jacobian matrix under consideration is strictly positive and hence non-singular. Then apply the Implicit Function Theorem to obtain that
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are continuously differentiable functions of
c
, denoted by

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{1}(c),\quad\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{2}(c).

Moreover, one can apply the same analysis in
[
13
]
for
c^{-1}V
and obtain a similar result in Application 1 (1.64) (1.65) in
[
13
]
for the regular case (also refer to the proof of Theorem 2.1 in
[
12
]
). Note that in their notation in the one-cut case
\tilde{M}^{(\infty)}(z)
found as (4.71) of
[
13
]
is reduced to

\tilde{M}^{(\infty)}(z)=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma(z)+\gamma(z)^{-1},&i(\gamma(z)^{-1}-\gamma(z))\\
i(\gamma(z)-\gamma(z)^{-1}),&\gamma(z)+\gamma(z)^{-1}\end{pmatrix},

which is equivalent to
N(z)
defined in (6.13-6.16) of
[
12
]
after rescaling the support from
[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}]
to
[-1,1]
.
This means that the recurrence coefficients can be estimated as

a_{j,n}=\frac{\alpha_{2}(c)-\alpha_{1}(c)}{4}+O(n^{-1}),\quad b_{j,n}=\frac{\alpha_{2}(c)+\alpha_{1}(c)}{2}+O(n^{-1}),

(329)

uniformly for all
j\in I_{n}^{(\alpha,\varepsilon)}
. Recall that
c=\frac{j}{n}
and
\alpha_{1}(c),\alpha_{2}(c)
are continuously differentiable functions. Then for all
j\in I_{n}^{(\alpha,\varepsilon)}
we have

|a_{j,n}-a_{j-1,n}|=O(n^{-1}),\quad|b_{j,n}-b_{j-1,n}|=O(n^{-1}).

Moreover, by the normalisation we have
\alpha_{1}(1)=-1,\alpha_{2}(1)=1
. Hence,

\lim_{j/n\to 1}a_{j,n}=\frac{1}{2},\quad\lim_{j/n\to 1}b_{j,n}=0.

From here we obtain that the edges of the fluctuations are at
x_{0}=-1
or
1
.
Now we can employ Theorem
2.2
and conclude Theorem
1.2
.
∎

Note that the strict convexity is not a necessary condition for the Jacobian to be non-singular nor the equilibrium measure to be supported on a single interval. Though it can be relaxed, convexity simplifies the argument. Furthermore, analyticity is only needed for the Riemann-Hilbert technique to obtain the asymptotics of recurrence coefficients. However, it is highly possible that (
329
) still holds for
V
having some degree of smoothness, without assuming analyticity. In the literature, the Riemann–Hilbert–
\bar{\partial}
method is able to deal with V with two Lipschitz continuous derivatives, see
[
37
]
. Their result Theorem 1(1) indicates that the recurrence coefficients should be slowly varying with
O(n^{-\frac{1}{3}}\log n)
, in a more general setup. Here we believe that Theorem
1.2
should still hold for
V
being a convex (or the equilibrium measure being supported in a single interval) and
C^{2+\varepsilon}(\mathbb{R})
(for some
\varepsilon>0
) potential satisfying (
8
). This requires a more detailed study about the potential theory and we leave it open.

9
Proof of Theorem
2.1

Assumption (
20
) allows us to approximate the Jacobi matrix
\mathcal{J}
by a Toeplitz matrix. Compared with Theorem
2.3
, we have more control of the tails of the diagonals in this case. Hence, a direct comparison with a Toeplitz operator is possible by exploring the algebra of the cumulant formula. In this section we are to prove Theorem
2.1
by fulfilling such an idea.

For clarity, throughout this section we let

\beta=\frac{\alpha+\varepsilon}{2},\quad\text{for some }\varepsilon>0\quad\text{such that }0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1.

(330)

As mentioned in the remark of Theorem
2.1
, we will give a proof with a weaker assumption (
20
). Without loss of generality we further assume

.\sup\limits_{j\geq n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}}|a_{j,n}-1|=O(n^{-2\beta}),\quad\sup\limits_{j\geq n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}}|b_{j,n}|=O(n^{-2\beta}).

(331)

The general case follows by translating and scaling the point process by
(x-b)/a
, where
a\coloneqq\lim_{n}a_{n,n}
and
b\coloneqq\lim_{n}b_{n-1,n}
.

9.1
Resolvent of a Toeplitz Operator

We will first consider a block semi-infinite matrix where the first block has the same entries as
(a_{j,n},b_{j,n})
for all
j<n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}
, and the second block has constant entries
(1,0)
, i.e.,

\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\coloneqq P_{n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}}\mathcal{J}P_{n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}}+Q_{n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}}\begin{pmatrix}0&1&&\\
1&0&1&\\
&1&0&1&\\
&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots\end{pmatrix}Q_{n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}}.

(332)

Take
x_{0}=-2
and define

\omega_{+}\coloneqq\frac{2-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}+\left(\left(2-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{2}-4\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2},\quad\omega_{-}\coloneqq\frac{2-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}-\left(\left(2-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{2}-4\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2},

(333)

where the square root is taken on the principal branch so that
|\omega_{+}|>|\omega_{-}|
. The right edge (
x_{0}=2
) can be done in the exact same way by swapping the definitions of
\omega_{+}
and
\omega_{-}
.

Since the second block of
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
is a Toeplitz operator, we can use the Wiener-Hopf factorisation to obtain the exact inverse formula entry-wise, for all
j,k\geq n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}+1

\left(\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\left(x_{0}+\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)\mathrm{Id}\right)^{-1}\right)_{j,k}=\frac{\left(-\omega_{+}\right)^{-|j-k|}-\left(-\omega_{-}\right)^{j+k-2(n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}+1)}}{\omega_{+}-\omega_{-}}.

(334)

One should compare this formula with Proposition
4.3
.

Note that

\omega_{+}=1+\left(-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+O(n^{-\alpha}),\quad\omega_{-}=1-\left(-\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+O(n^{-\alpha}).

(335)

From (
334
) and (
335
), we see that for all
j,k\geq n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}+1

\left|\left(\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\left(x_{0}+\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)\mathrm{Id}\right)^{-1}\right)_{j,k}\right|\leq Cn^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}e^{-dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}|j-k|}.

(336)

One convenient consequence of this estimate is the following lemma.

Lemma 9.1
.

There exist a constant
C_{0}>0
, such that for any integers
m_{1},m_{2}\geq n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}+1
, we have

\left\|P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\left(x_{0}+\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)\mathrm{Id}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{C_{0}n^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}}|m_{1}-m_{2}|}.

(337)

Proof.

For
m_{1}\leq m_{2}
,
P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}}=0
and the norm on left-hand side is zero. The inequality holds trivially. We now consider the case where
m_{1}>m_{2}
.

Recall for any linear Hilbert-Schmidt operator
A
, the Hilbert-Schmidt norm
\|A\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j,k}|(A)_{j,k}|^{2}
. Then we can estimate by (
336
)

\left\|P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\left(x_{0}+\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)\mathrm{Id}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sum_{j=m_{2}+1}^{m_{1}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}C^{2}n^{\alpha}e^{-2dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}|j-k|}\\
\leq\sum_{j=m_{2}+1}^{m_{1}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}C^{2}n^{\alpha}e^{-2dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}|l|}\leq 2\sum_{j=m_{2}+1}^{m_{1}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}C^{2}n^{\alpha}e^{-2dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}l}=\frac{2C^{2}n^{\alpha}(m_{1}-m_{2})}{1-e^{-2dn^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}},

(338)

where the second line is by adding more terms in the sum to make the series a power series. Note that
1-e^{-x}\geq e^{-1}x
for all
x\in[0,1]
, we can further estimate (
338
) to be

\left\|P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\left(x_{0}+\frac{\eta}{n^{\alpha}}\right)\mathrm{Id}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{2eC^{2}}{d}n^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}}(m_{1}-m_{2}).

(339)

This completes the proof.
∎

9.2
Lemmas of Proof of Theorem
2.1

We consider test function
f(x)=\sum_{r=1}^{2M}c_{r}\frac{1}{x-\eta_{r}}
as described in (
43
) for some
\eta\in\{x+iy|x\in\mathbb{R},y\neq 0\}
. Let us define

\widetilde{F}\coloneqq\sum_{r=1}^{2M}c_{r}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}.

(340)

Denote the Jacobi matrix of the OPE with recurrence coefficients with
\{a_{j,n},b_{j,n}\}
satisfying (
331
) to be
\mathcal{J}
. Define

F\coloneqq\sum_{r=1}^{2M}c_{r}\left(\mathcal{J}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}.

(341)

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
. Let
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
m_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
m_{1}>n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}+1
. Then there exists a
C_{l}>0
such that

\left\|Q_{m_{1}+ln^{\beta}}\widetilde{F}^{l}P_{m_{1}}\right\|_{1}\leq C_{l}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(342)

\left\|P_{m_{1}-ln^{\beta}}\widetilde{F}^{l}Q_{m_{1}}\right\|_{1}\leq C_{l}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}

(343)

Proof.

Repeat the proof of (
174
) in Lemma
5.4
taking
N=\infty
, using (
336
) and one gets (
342
). Repeat the proof of (
184
) in Lemma
5.5
summing
j
from
1
and
k
up to
\infty
, using (
336
) and one gets (
343
).
∎

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
. Assume (
331
) holds for all
j>m_{2}-2n^{\beta}
. For
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
m_{2}\geq n-n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}+2n^{\beta}+2
,
2n^{\beta}<m_{2}<m_{1}
and
m_{1}-m_{2}=O(n^{\beta})
, we have, as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(344)

Proof.

Let us define

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}

(345)

\displaystyle F^{(r)}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{J}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}.

(346)

By triangle inequality we have

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\leq\sum_{r}|c_{r}|\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}^{(r)}-F^{(r)}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}.

(347)

Now we are going to study the trace norm for any
r
. For a cleaner notation, let

\mathcal{A}=\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}},\quad\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{J}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}.

It is sufficient to estimate
\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}
. Note that estimates about
\mathcal{A}^{-1}
are discussed in Section
9.1
.

We use resolvent identity and rewrite
\mathcal{B}^{-1}=\mathcal{A}^{-1}+\left(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right)
to get

P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}=P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\\
=P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}+P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right)\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}.

(348)

Note that, by the fact that
\mathrm{Id}=P_{m_{2}}+Q_{m_{2}}
the second summand on the right-hand side can be written as

P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right)P_{m_{2}}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}+P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}.

Rearrange the formula (
348
) and we get,

P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right)\\
=P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}+P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right)P_{m_{2}}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}.

(349)

Note that, by Lemma
9.2
, we have
\left\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}-Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O(e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}})
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}
is three diagonal, we have
\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}=Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}
. Also by Lemma
9.2
, we have
\left\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}-Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\right\|_{1}=O(e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}})
. Also note that
\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}\|_{\infty}=O(n^{\alpha})
,
\|\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B}\|_{\infty}=O(1)
. Hence, the first summand on the right-hand side of (
349
) can be estimated to be

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
=\left\|P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}+O\left(n^{\alpha}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right).

(350)

Similarly, we commute
P_{m_{1}}
from left to right on the right-hand side of (
350
) with exponentially small error. Precisely, by Lemma
9.2
, we have
\left\|P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}-P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}=O(e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}).
Since
\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}
is three diagonal, we have
P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)=P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}+1}.
Also by Lemma
9.2
, we have
\left\|P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}-P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{1}+2n^{\beta}+1}\right\|_{1}=O(e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}})
. Note that
\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}\|_{\infty}=O(n^{\alpha})
,
\|\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B}\|_{\infty}=O(1)
. Also note that the projections are commutative, i.e.,
P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}}=Q_{m_{2}}P_{m_{1}}
. Hence, (
350
) can be further estimated to be as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=\\
\left\|P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{1}+2n^{\beta}+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
+O\left(n^{\alpha}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right).

(351)

Now use the trace norm inequality
\|ABC\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{2}\|B\|_{\infty}\|C\|_{2}
and we get as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\leq\\
\left\|P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\left\|P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{1}+2n^{\beta}+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{2}\\
+O\left(n^{\alpha}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right).

(352)

Recall that we assume
m_{1}-m_{2}=O(n^{\beta})
. By Lemma
9.1
, we have as
n\to\infty

\displaystyle\left\|P_{m_{1}}Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right\|_{2}=O\left(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\frac{\beta}{2}}\right)

(353)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{1}+2n^{\beta}+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{2}=O\left(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\frac{\beta}{2}}\right).

(354)

Since (
331
) holds for all
j>m_{2}-2n^{\beta}
by assumption, we have as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)P_{m_{1}+n^{\beta}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\right\|_{\infty}=O\left(n^{-2\beta}\right).

(355)

Plug (
353
), (
354
) and (
355
) into (
352
) to get as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(356)

For the second summand of the right-hand side of (
349
), we first use the resolvent identity and then continue with the same argument as (
351
) to commute
Q_{m_{2}}
from right to left to obtain that as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right)P_{m_{2}}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{2}}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
=\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B}\right)Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{2}}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)P_{m_{2}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
+O\left(n^{2\alpha}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right).

(357)

Use the trace norm inequality
\|ABCD\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}\|B\|_{2}\|C\|_{\infty}\|D\|_{2}
to get

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B}\right)Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{2}}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)P_{m_{2}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B}\right)Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{2}}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\right\|_{2}\left\|P_{m_{2}}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\left\|P_{m_{2}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{2}

(358)

In (
358
), two operator norms are of order
O(n^{\alpha-2\beta})
and
O(n^{-2\beta})
respectively by the assumption that (
331
) holds for all
j>m_{2}-2n^{\beta}
; two Hilbert-Schmidt norms are both of order
O(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\frac{\beta}{2}})
by Lemma
9.1
. Hence, as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{B}\right)Q_{m_{2}-2n^{\beta}-1}\mathcal{A}^{-1}P_{m_{2}}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)P_{m_{2}+1}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{\frac{5\alpha}{2}-3\beta}\right).

(359)

Plug (
359
) into (
357
) to get

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\right)P_{m_{2}}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{\frac{5\alpha}{2}-3\beta}\right).

(360)

We now use (
356
) and (
360
) to estimate the trace norm of (
349
), and we have as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right)\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(361)

Note that we use the assumption
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
to determine
\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta>\frac{5\alpha}{2}-3\beta
.

By Lemma
9.2
,

\left\|\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}=\left\|\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}+O\left(e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right).

(362)

Using the operator norm inequality we have

\left\|\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}.

(363)

Since (
331
) holds for all
j>m_{2}-2n^{\beta}
, we have
\left\|\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\right\|_{\infty}=O(n^{-2\beta})
, as
n\to\infty
. Recall that
\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}\|_{\infty}=O(n^{\alpha})
. Now we have

\left\|\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}=O\left(n^{\alpha-2\beta}\right).

(364)

Recall
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta
, and hence (
364
) is of order
o(1)
. Then for large
n
,

\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\left\|\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}}.

(365)

Moreover, by trace norm inequality
\|AB\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{1}\|B\|_{\infty}
, we have

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right)\right\|_{1}\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}Q_{m_{2}}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}.

(366)

Plug (
361
),(
364
) and (
365
) into (
366
) to obtain as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(367)

Plug this estimate back into (
347
) and we conclude as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(368)

∎

Lemma above also implies the following by induction.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Assume (
331
) holds. We have, for any
k=0,1,2,\dots,m

\left\|P_{n+mn^{\beta}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{k}Q_{n}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{k\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(369)

Proof.

First note that the following operator norms are bounded

\sup_{n>0}\{n^{-\alpha}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{\infty},n^{-\alpha}\|F\|_{\infty}\}<\infty.

(370)

Take
m_{1}\equiv n+mn^{\beta}
and
m_{2}\equiv n
to shorten the notation.

Consider the induction argument. For
k=0
, (
369
) is reduced to Lemma
9.3
. For any
k\geq 0
, we use triangle inequality to obtain

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{k+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)\widetilde{F}^{k+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}+\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)\left(F^{k+1}-\widetilde{F}^{k+1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}.

(371)

Use Lemma
9.2
and we estimate the first summand on the right-hand side of (
371
) to be, as
n\to\infty
,

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)\widetilde{F}^{k+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)Q_{m_{2}-(k+1)n^{\beta}}\widetilde{F}^{k+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}+O\left(n^{\alpha}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right).

(372)

By the trace norm inequality
\|AB\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{1}\|B\|_{\infty}
, we have

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)Q_{m_{2}-(k+1)n^{\beta}}\widetilde{F}^{k+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)Q_{m_{2}-(k+1)n^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\left\|\widetilde{F}^{k+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}.

(373)

Then use Lemma
9.3
to estimate the trace norm above to be of order
O(n^{3\alpha/2-\beta})
. By (
370
), the operator norm is of order
O\left(n^{(k+1)\alpha}\right)
. Plug the estimate of (
373
) back to (
372
) to obtain, as
n\to\infty

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)\widetilde{F}^{k+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{(k+1)\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(374)

For the second summand on the right-hand side of (
371
) we use the telescopic sum,

F^{k+1}-\widetilde{F}^{k+1}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}F^{l}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\widetilde{F}^{k-l},

the fact that
Q_{m_{2}}+P_{m_{2}}=\mathrm{Id}
, and triangle inequality to obtain

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)\left(F^{k+1}-\widetilde{F}^{k+1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}\left(Q_{m_{2}}+P_{m_{2}}\right)\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}Q_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}+\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}P_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\right).

(375)

Use the trace norm inequality
\|ABC\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{1}\|B\|_{\infty}\|C\|_{\infty}
to obtain an estimate of the first term on the right-hand side of (
375
),

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}Q_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}.

(376)

The induction hypothesis for all
l=0,\dots,k
implies the first term on the right-hand side of (
376
) to be

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{l\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(377)

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
376
), let us define,
Let us define

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}

(378)

\displaystyle F^{(r)}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{J}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}.

(379)

Recall the definition of
\widetilde{F}
and
F
in (
340
) and (
341
). We apply the triangle inequality and the resolvent identity to obtain

\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{r}|c_{r}|\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\left(F^{(r)}-\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\right)\right\|_{\infty}=\sum_{r}|c_{r}|\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)F^{(r)}\right\|_{\infty}.

(380)

Use Lemma
9.2
, and we get

\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)F^{(r)}\right\|_{\infty}=\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\widetilde{F}^{(r)}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)F^{(r)}\right\|_{\infty}+O\left(n^{\alpha}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right).

(381)

By the operator norm inequality
\|AB\|_{\infty}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}\|B\|_{\infty}
we obtain,

\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\widetilde{F}^{(r)}Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)F^{(r)}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\left\|F^{(r)}\right\|_{\infty}.

(382)

By the assumption that (
331
) holds for all
j>m_{2}-2n^{\beta}
, we get
\left\|Q_{m_{2}-n^{\beta}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)\right\|_{\infty}=O(n^{-2\beta})
. Together with the fact that
\|F^{(r)}\|_{\infty}=O\left(n^{\alpha}\right)
and
\|\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\|_{\infty}=O\left(n^{\alpha}\right)
, we estimate (
382
) to be of order
O(n^{2\alpha-2\beta})
. Plugging it into (
381
) and further back into (
380
), we get

\left\|Q_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\right\|_{\infty}=O\left(n^{2\alpha-2\beta}\right).

(383)

The last term on the right-hand side of (
376
) is estimated to be
\left\|\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}=O\left(n^{(k-l)\alpha}\right)
. Hence, together with (
377
) and (
383
), (
376
) is estimated to be

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}Q_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{(k+2)\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-3\beta}\right).

(384)

Now we turn to the second term on the right-hand side of (
375
). Use Lemma
9.2
,

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}P_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
=\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}P_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)Q_{m_{2}-(k-l)n^{\beta}}\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}+O\left(n^{(l+2)\alpha}e^{-d^{\prime}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right).

(385)

Use the operator norm inequality
\|ABC\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}\|B\|_{1}\|C\|_{\infty}
to obtain

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}P_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)Q_{m_{2}-(k-l)n^{\beta}}\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|P_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)Q_{m_{2}-(k-l)n^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\left\|\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}.

(386)

The operator norms are estimated to be
\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}\right\|_{\infty}=O\left(n^{(l+1)\alpha}\right)
and
\left\|\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{\infty}=O\left(n^{(k-l)\alpha}\right)
. The trace norm can be estimated by Lemma
9.3
to be
\left\|P_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)Q_{m_{2}-(k-l)n^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right)
. Plug these three estimates into (
386
). Then we get an estimate of (
385
) to be

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{l}P_{m_{2}}\left(F-\widetilde{F}\right)\widetilde{F}^{k-l}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}\\
=O\left(n^{(k+1)\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(387)

Plug (
384
) and (
387
) back into (
375
), and we estimate the second summand on the right-hand side of (
371
) to be

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)\left(F^{k+1}-\widetilde{F}^{k+1}\right)Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{(k+1)\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(388)

Here we use the assumption
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<1
to deduce
O\left(n^{(k+2)\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-3\beta}\right)=o\left(n^{(k+1)\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right)
.

Plugging (
374
) and (
388
) into (
371
), we obtain

\left\|P_{m_{1}}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{k+1}Q_{m_{2}}\right\|_{1}=O\left(n^{(k+1)\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right).

(389)

This concludes the induction argument.
∎

9.3
Proof of Theorem
2.1

The essential step of Theorem
2.1
is the following proposition. Recall that
\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(\mathcal{A})
is the
m
-th cumulant for a linear operator
\mathcal{A}
, defined as (
64
).

Proposition 9.1
.

Assume that the assumptions in Theorem
2.1
are satisfied. Then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-m\alpha}\left|\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(\widetilde{F})-\mathcal{C}_{m}^{(n)}(F)\right|=0.

(390)

Proof.

First note that the following operator norms are bounded

\sup_{n>0}\{n^{-\alpha}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{\infty},n^{-\alpha}\|F\|_{\infty}\}\eqqcolon C_{op}<\infty.

(391)

Since both sums are finite in the cumulant formula (
64
), it is sufficient to show that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\widetilde{F})^{l_{1}}P_{n}\dots(\widetilde{F})^{l_{j}}P_{n}-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\widetilde{F}^{m}P_{n})-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(F)^{l_{1}}P_{n}\dots(F)^{l_{j}}P_{n}+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(F^{m}P_{n})=o(n^{m\alpha})

(392)

Then we rewrite the left-hand side of (
392
) by Lemma
5.6
.

Then writing
\widetilde{F}^{l}-F^{l}=\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}F^{l-1-k}(\widetilde{F}-F)\widetilde{F}^{k}
, by Lemma
9.2
, we commute
Q_{n}
from the right to left to get

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left((\widetilde{F}^{l_{k}}-F^{l_{k}})P_{n}\widetilde{F}^{l_{k+1}}\dots P_{n}\widetilde{F}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\widetilde{F}^{l_{1}+\dots+l_{k-1}}Q_{n}\right)\right|\leq l_{k}C_{op}^{m-1}n^{(m-1)\alpha}\|(\widetilde{F}-F)P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}+R_{n},

(393)

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(F^{l_{k}}P_{n}\dots P_{n}F^{l_{i}}P_{n}(\widetilde{F}^{l_{i+1}}-F^{l_{i+1}})P_{n}\dots\widetilde{F}^{l_{j}}P_{n}\widetilde{F}^{l_{1}+\dots+l_{k-1}}Q_{n}\right)\right|\\
\leq l_{i+1}C_{op}^{m-1}n^{(m-1)\alpha}\|(\widetilde{F}-F)P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}+R_{n},

(394)

\left|\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(F^{l_{k}}P_{n}\dots P_{n}F^{l_{j}}P_{n}(\widetilde{F}^{l_{1}+\dots+l_{k-1}}-F^{l_{1}+\dots+l_{k-1}})Q_{n}\right)\right|\\
\leq C_{op}^{m-(l_{1}+\dots+l_{k-1})}n^{(m-(l_{1}+\dots+l_{k-1}))\alpha}\|P_{n}(\widetilde{F}^{l_{1}+\dots+l_{k-1}}-F^{l_{1}+\dots+l_{k-1}})Q_{n}\|_{1}+R_{n},

(395)

where
R_{n}=O(n^{m\alpha}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}})
is exponentially small.

Define

\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\coloneqq\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1},\quad F^{(r)}\coloneqq\left(\mathcal{J}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}.

(396)

Use the triangle inequality to obtain

\|(\widetilde{F}-F)P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{1}\leq\sum_{r=1}^{2M}|c_{r}|\left\|(\widetilde{F}^{(r)}-F^{(r)})P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}.

(397)

Recall that for any linear operator
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, we have the resolvent identity
\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}=\mathcal{B}^{-1}(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A})\mathcal{A}^{-1}
. Further, we also have
\mathcal{B}^{-1}=\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}
. Combine these two formulas to get,

\mathcal{A}^{-1}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}=\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A}\right)\right)\mathcal{A}^{-1}(\mathcal{B}-\mathcal{A})\mathcal{A}^{-1}.

Take
\mathcal{A}=\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}=\left(\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\right)^{-1}
and
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{J}-x_{0}-\frac{\eta_{r}}{n^{\alpha}}=\left(F^{(r)}\right)^{-1}
. Rewrite each summand of the right-hand side of (
397
) to be

\left\|(\widetilde{F}^{(r)}-F^{(r)})P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}=\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-F^{(r)}\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right)\widetilde{F}^{(r)}\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\widetilde{F}^{(r)}P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}.

(398)

Recall that
\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
is tri-diagonal and we have for any integer
m_{1}

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)P_{m_{1}}=P_{m_{1}+1}\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)P_{m_{1}}.

(399)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)Q_{m_{1}}=Q_{m_{1}-1}\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)Q_{m_{1}}.

(400)

Use Lemma
9.2
to get that
\widetilde{F}^{(r)}
commutes with the projection operator
P_{m_{1}}
and
Q_{m_{1}}
with an exponential small error. That is

\left\|(\widetilde{F}^{(r)}-F^{(r)})P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\\
\leq\Bigg\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-F^{(r)}\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right)P_{n+2n^{\beta}+1}Q_{n-(m+2)n^{\beta}-1}\widetilde{F}^{(r)}P_{n+n^{\beta}+1}Q_{n-(m+1)n^{\beta}-1}\\
\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)P_{n+n^{\beta}}Q_{n-(m+1)n^{\beta}}\widetilde{F}^{(r)}P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\Bigg\|_{1}+R_{n},

(401)

where
R_{n}=O(n^{m\alpha}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}})
is exponentially small. Use the trace norm inequality
\|ABCD\|_{1}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}\|B\|_{2}\|C\|_{\infty}\|D\|_{2}
and (
398
) to obtain

\left\|(\widetilde{F}^{(r)}-F^{(r)})P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}\leq\|(\mathrm{Id}-F^{(r)}\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)P_{n+2n^{\beta}+1}Q_{n-(m+2)n^{\beta}-1}\|_{\infty}\\
\|\widetilde{F}^{(r)}P_{n+n^{\beta}+1}Q_{n-(m+1)n^{\beta}-1}\|_{2}\|\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)P_{n+n^{\beta}}Q_{n-(m+1)n^{\beta}}\|_{\infty}\|\widetilde{F}^{(r)}P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{2}+R_{n},

(402)

where
R_{n}=O\left(n^{\alpha+2\beta}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right)
is exponentially small. By the assumption (
331
) and the fact that
\|F^{(r)}\|_{\infty}=O(n^{\alpha})
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}-\mathcal{J}\right)P_{n+n^{\beta}}Q_{n-(m+1)n^{\beta}}\right\|_{\infty}=

\displaystyle O(n^{-2\beta}),

(403)

\displaystyle\left\|F^{(r)}\left(\mathcal{J}-\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\right)P_{n+2n^{\beta}+1}Q_{n-(m+2)n^{\beta}-1}\right\|_{\infty}=

\displaystyle O(n^{\alpha-2\beta}).

(404)

Use Lemma
9.1
to obtain

\|\widetilde{F}^{(r)}P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}=O(n^{\beta+\frac{3\alpha}{2}}).

(405)

Recall that
R_{n}
is exponentially small for
n
large. Plug (
403
), (
404
) and (
405
) into (
402
) to obtain, as
n\to\infty
,

\left\|(\widetilde{F}^{(r)}-F^{(r)})P_{n}Q_{n-mn^{\beta}}\right\|_{1}=O(n^{-\beta+\frac{3\alpha}{2}}).

(406)

The estimate (
406
), together with (
397
), implies that (
393
) and (
394
) are both of order
O\left(n^{m\alpha-\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)
.

For (
395
), consider any
l=1,2,\dots,m
. Recall the telescoping sum

\widetilde{F}^{l}-F^{l}=\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}\widetilde{F}^{l-k-1}\left(\widetilde{F}-F\right)F^{k}

(407)

Apply Lemma
9.2
to obtain

\left\|P_{n}(\widetilde{F}^{l}-F^{l})Q_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}(n^{\alpha}C_{op})^{l-k-1}\left\|P_{n+(l-k-1)n^{\beta}}(\widetilde{F}-F)F^{k}Q_{n}\right\|_{1}+R_{n},

(408)

where
R_{n}=O\left(n^{\alpha+2\beta}e^{-d_{0}n^{\beta-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right)
is exponentially small. We estimate (
408
), by Lemma
9.4
, to be

\displaystyle\|P_{n}(\widetilde{F}^{l}-F^{l})Q_{n}\|_{1}=O\left(n^{(l-1)\alpha+\frac{3\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right)

(409)

This shows that (
395
) is of order
O\left(n^{m\alpha+\frac{\alpha}{2}-\beta}\right)
.

In summary we have shown that (
393
), (
394
) and (
395
) are all of order of
O\left(n^{m\alpha-\beta+\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)
. Hence, by assumption
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<2
, we conclude that (
392
) holds. Plug this into the cumulant formula (
64
) and we complete the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
2.1
.

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

First consider test functions like
f(x)=\sum_{r=1}^{M}d_{r}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(x-\lambda_{r})^{-1}
for
d_{r}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\lambda_{r})>0
,i.e., (
43
) .

Note that by Lemma
5.6
, we can remove the first block of
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
in the cumulant expression
\mathcal{C}_{m}(\widetilde{F})
for all
m\geq 2
. Then
\mathcal{C}_{m}(\widetilde{F})
can be regarded as the cumulant for the Chebyshev polynomial of the second kind at the edge as described in beginning of Subsection
9.1
of order
n^{\beta+\varepsilon/2}
at the mesoscopic scale of
\frac{\alpha}{\beta+\varepsilon/2}=\frac{\alpha}{\alpha/2+\varepsilon}\in(0,2)
. Apply Theorem
2.3
and get the following convergence in distribution as
n\to\infty
.

This implies the convergence of the cumulants

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-m\alpha}\mathcal{C}_{m}(\widetilde{F})=\begin{cases}\sigma_{f}^{2},&\quad m=2\\
0,&\quad m>2\end{cases},

(410)

where
\sigma_{f}^{2}
is the variance in Theorem
2.3
.
Then Proposition
9.1
implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-m\alpha}\mathcal{C}_{m}(F)=\begin{cases}\sigma_{f}^{2},&\quad m=2\\
0,&\quad m>2.\end{cases}

(411)

This implies that

X_{f_{\alpha,x_{0}}}^{(n)}-\mathbb{E}[X_{f_{\alpha,x_{0}}}^{(n)}]\to\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{f}^{2}),

(412)

where
X_{f_{\alpha,x_{0}}}^{(n)}
is the mesoscopic linear statistics of for OPEs whose recurrence coefficients satisfy (
331
).

Now recall the argument in Section
4.2
. Use the exact same proof of the last step of Theorem
2.3
in Section
4.2
and we extend (
412
) to the test functions
f\in C_{c}^{1}
.
∎

10
Examples

Theorems
2.1
,
2.2
and
2.3
can be used to obtain the asymptotics of mesoscopic fluctuations at the edges of many classes of OPEs, that are known in the literature. Here we present some of the interesting examples with both continuous and discrete measures. We include classical examples as well as uncommon but still popular ones.

10.1
Laguerre Unitary Ensemble

The orthogonality measure of scaled Laguerre polynomials is given by

d\mu(x)=x^{\gamma}e^{-nx}dx,\quad x\geq 0,

for some parameter
\gamma>-1
.
The recurrence relation reads

xp_{j}(x)=\frac{\sqrt{(j+1)(j+1+\gamma)}}{n}p_{j+1}(x)+\frac{2j+\gamma+1}{n}p_{j}(x)+\frac{\sqrt{j(j+\gamma)}}{n}p_{j-1}(x)

and hence the recurrence coefficients are

a_{j,n}=\frac{\sqrt{j(j+\gamma)}}{n}\quad,\quad b_{j,n}=\frac{2j+\gamma+1}{n}

(413)

Figure 3
:
The equilibrium measure of Laguerre Unitary Ensemble.

Clearly, Condition
2.1
is satisfied for all recurrence coefficients with indices in the following set
I
,

I=\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}:\frac{j}{n}\to 1\right\}.

(414)

At the left edge,
b_{n-1,n}-2\sqrt{a_{n,n}a_{n-1,n}}=\frac{\gamma^{2}+1}{4n^{2}}+O\left(n^{-3}\right)
, by Taylor approximation. Take
x_{0}=0
. We compute that for all
j\in I_{n,m}^{(\beta)}

a_{j,n}a_{j-2,n}-a_{j-1,n}^{2}=-\frac{1}{n^{2}}+O(n^{-4}),

(415)

(b_{j-1,n}-x_{0}-a_{j,n})a_{j-2,n}-(b_{j-2,n}-x_{0}-a_{j-1,n})a_{j-1,n}=-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2n^{3}}+O(n^{\beta-4}).

(416)

By Theorem
2.3
we conclude that the mesoscopic CLT holds for all
0<\frac{\alpha}{2}<\beta<\frac{\alpha+1}{3}<1
. Taking
\beta\to\frac{\alpha}{2}
. We conclude that the mesoscopic CLT holds for all
0<\alpha<2
.

At the right edge,
b_{n-1,n}+2\sqrt{a_{n,n}a_{n-1,n}}=4+(2\gamma-2)n^{-1}+O(n^{-2})
, by Taylor approximation. Take
x_{0}=4
, apply Theorem
2.2
and we conclude that the asymptotics of the edge fluctuations holds for all
\alpha\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
.

10.2
Gaussian Unitary Ensemble

The orthogonality measure of scaled Hermite polynomials is given by

d\mu(x)=e^{-nx^{2}/2}dx,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}.

The recurrence coefficients read

a_{j,n}=\sqrt{\frac{j}{n}}\quad,\quad b_{j,n}=0.

(417)

Figure 4
:
The equilibrium measure of Gaussian Unitary Ensemble.

Clearly, Condition
2.1
is satisfied for all recurrence coefficients with indices in the following set
I
,

I=\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}:\frac{j}{n}\to 1\right\}.

(418)

Note that
b_{n-1,n}\pm 2\sqrt{a_{n,n}a_{n-1,n}}=\pm 2+O(n^{-1})
. Take
x_{0}=-2
or
2
, apply Theorem
2.2
and we conclude that the asymptotics of the edge fluctuations holds for all
\alpha\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
.

10.3
Jacobi Unitary Ensemble

The orthogonality measure of Jacobi polynomials is given by

d\mu(x)=(2-x)^{\gamma_{1}}(x+2)^{\gamma_{2}}dx,\quad x\in[-2,2],

for some parameter
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}>-1
. The recurrence coefficients read

a_{j}=\sqrt{\frac{16j(j+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2})(j+\gamma_{1})(j+\gamma_{2})}{(2j+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}-1)(2j+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2})^{2}(2j+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+1)}},\quad b_{j}=\frac{2(\gamma_{2}^{2}-\gamma_{1}^{2})}{(2j+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2})(2j+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+2)}.

(419)

Note that as
j\to\infty
,

a_{j}=1+\frac{1-2\gamma_{1}^{2}-2\gamma_{2}^{2}}{8j^{2}}+O(j^{-3}),\quad b_{j}=\frac{\gamma_{2}^{2}-\gamma_{1}^{2}}{2j^{2}}+O(j^{-3}).

The equilibrium measure is given the following figure.

Figure 5
:
The equilibrium measure of Jacobi Unitary Ensemble.

The edges of fluctuations are at
x_{0}=2
or
-2
. Hence, by Theorem
2.3
or
2.1
we have the mesoscopic fluctuations at the edges hold for all
\alpha\in(0,2)
. Note that for the Chebyshev polynomial of the second kind we have
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=-1/2
,
a_{j}=1
and
b_{j}=0
.

10.4
Freud Weight

The orthogonality measure for Freud polynomials is given by Freud weight

d\mu_{n}(x)=e^{-n|x|^{\gamma}}dx,\quad\gamma>0,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}.

The recurrence is shown in
[
36
,
27
]
that

a_{j,n}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma}{2})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma+1}{2})}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\left(\frac{j}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(1+res_{\gamma}(j))\quad\text{and }b_{j,n}=0,

(420)

where
\Gamma
is the Gamma function and

res_{\gamma}(j)=\begin{cases}O(j^{-2}),&\quad\gamma\geq 2\text{ or }0<\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2},\\
O(j^{-\gamma}),&\quad 1<\gamma\leq 2,\\
O(j^{-1}(\log j)^{-2}),&\quad\gamma=1,\\
O(j^{-1/\gamma}),&\quad\gamma\geq 2\text{ or }\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1\end{cases},\quad\text{as }j\to\infty.

(421)

Note that its Jacobi operator is essentially self-adjoint if and only if
\gamma\geq 1
. In such case, the moment problem is determinate. Note that for
0<\gamma<1
, the set of Freud orthonormal polynomials is not dense in
L^{2}(\mu_{n})
. The moment problem is indeterminate.

Clearly, Condition
2.1
is satisfied for all recurrence coefficients with indices in the following set
I
,

I=\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}:\frac{j}{n}\to 1\right\}.

(422)

Note that
b_{n-1,n}\pm 2\sqrt{a_{n,n}a_{n-1,n}}=\pm\left(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma}{2})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma+1}{2})}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}+O(n^{-1})
. For both edges
x_{0}=-\left(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma}{2})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma+1}{2})}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}
or
\left(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma}{2})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma+1}{2})}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}
, apply Theorem
2.2
and we conclude that the asymptotics of the edge fluctuations holds for all
\alpha\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
.

In particular, for
\gamma=2
, the Freud weight is reduced to be the Hermite case.

10.5
Tricomi-Carlitz Polynomial Ensemble

Next, we consider the Tricomi-Carlitz polynomials. Their zero distributions were first studied by
[
20
,
21
]
. We mention this example to emphasise that the edge of fluctuations may not coincide with the edge of the equilibrium measure. Take
\gamma>1
. The orthogonal measure
\nu^{(\gamma)}
is a step function with jumps at the points

\displaystyle\nu^{(\gamma)}(x)=\frac{(k+\gamma)^{k-1}e^{-k}}{k!}\qquad\text{ at }x=\pm(k+\gamma)^{-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad k=0,1,\dots.

It gives a discrete polynomial
f_{n}^{(\gamma)}
. To obtain a reasonable limit we need to rescale
x
by
n^{-1/2}
, see examples
4.7
in
[
29
]
. That is we have a scaled measure

\displaystyle\mu^{(\gamma)}(x)\coloneqq\nu^{(\gamma)}(\sqrt{n}x)=\frac{(k+\gamma)^{k-1}e^{-k}}{k!}\qquad\text{ at }x=\pm(k+\gamma)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{n},\qquad k=0,1,\dots.

The scaled polynomial is given by
f_{n}^{(\alpha)}(n^{-1/2}x)
.
However, its recurrence coefficients admit a simpler form

\displaystyle a_{j,n}=\sqrt{\frac{jn}{(j+\gamma-1)(j+\gamma)}}\quad,\quad b_{j,n}=0

(423)

Figure 6
:
The equilibrium measure of Tricomi-Carlitz Polynomial Ensemble.

Clearly, Condition
2.1
is satisfied for all recurrence coefficients with indices in the following set
I
,

I=\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}:\frac{j}{n}\to 1\right\}.

(424)

For both edges
b_{n-1,n}\pm 2\sqrt{a_{n,n}a_{n-1,n}}=\pm 2+O(n^{-1})
, by Taylor approximation. Take
x_{0}=-2
or
2
, apply Theorem
2.2
and we conclude that the asymptotics of the edge fluctuations holds for all
\alpha\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
.

Note that in this example the equilibrium measure is supported on
\mathbb{R}
. As is shown by
[
18
]
(cf. (
14
)),
\pm 2
are the global fluctuation edges. Points that are not in
[-2,2]
are said to be saturated. The resolvent argument used in this paper can also be adapted to show
that mesoscopic linear statistics supported strictly inside these
saturated regions have asymptotically vanishing variance.

10.6
Weight with Logarithm-Singularity

Deift and Piorkowski in the work
[
14
]
consider orthogonal polynomials with orthogonality measure by

d\mu(x)=\log\left(\frac{2}{1-x}\right),\qquad x\in[-1,1).

They show the asymptotics of its recurrence coefficients to be

a_{j}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{16j^{2}}-\frac{3}{32j^{2}\log^{2}(j)}+O\left(\frac{1}{j^{2}\log^{3}(j)}\right),\quad b_{j}=\frac{1}{4j^{2}}-\frac{3}{16j^{2}\log^{2}(j)}+O\left(\frac{1}{j^{2}\log^{3}(j)}\right).

Hence for both edges,
b_{j-1}\pm 2\sqrt{a_{j}a_{j-1}}=\pm 1+O(j^{-2})
. Take
x_{0}=-1
or
1
, apply Theorem
2.1
and we conclude that the asymptotics of the edge fluctuations holds for all
\alpha\in(0,2)
.

10.7
Krawtchouk Polynomial Ensemble

Given
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
p\in(0,1)
. The Krawtchouk polynomial
k_{j}(x;p,K)
is a discrete polynomial on
\{0,1,\dots,K\}
. The orthogonality weight
\nu
is

\nu(x)=\binom{K}{x}p^{x}(1-p)^{K-x},\qquad x=0,1,\dots,K.

The recurrence coefficients of the Krawtchouk polynomials are

a_{j}=\sqrt{(K-j+1)jp(1-p)},\quad b_{j}=(K-j)p+j(1-p).

The OPE with the Krawtchouk weight describes uniformly
distributed domino tilings of the Aztec diamond, see
[
23
]
. Here
K
is related to the size of the diamond and it is particularly interesting to let
K
go to infinity. Following
[
23
]
, we consider the case where
\frac{K}{n}\to t
for
t\geq 2
as
n\to\infty
. Of interest are the scaled polynomials
k_{j}(nx;p,K)
. Then the scaled orthogonal weight
\nu
is

\mu(x)=\binom{K}{nx}p^{nx}(1-p)^{K-nx},\qquad x=0,\frac{1}{n},\frac{2}{n},\dots,\frac{K}{n}.

The scaled Krawtchouk polynomials have the recurrence coefficients,

a_{j,n}=\sqrt{\frac{(K-j+1)jp(1-p)}{n^{2}}},\quad b_{j}=\frac{(K-j)p+j(1-p)}{n}.

Clearly, Condition
2.1
is satisfied for all recurrence coefficients with indices in the following set
I
,

I=\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}:\frac{j}{n}\to 1\right\}.

(425)

For both edges
b_{n-1,n}\pm 2\sqrt{a_{n,n}a_{n-1,n}}=(t-2)p+1\pm 2\sqrt{(t-1)p(1-p)}+O(n^{-1})
, by Taylor approximation. Take
x_{0}=(t-2)p+1\pm(t-1)\sqrt{p(1-p)}
, apply Theorem
2.2
and we conclude that the asymptotics of the edge fluctuations holds for all
\alpha\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
.

10.8
Hahn Polynomial Ensemble

Given
a,b>-1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
. The Hahn orthonormal weight is defined as

\nu_{N}^{(a,b)}(x)=\binom{a+x}{x}\binom{b+N-x}{N-x},\quad,x=0,1,\dots,N.

(426)

The Hahn ensemble appears in the lozenge tilings of a hexagon with uniform weights. The parameters
a,b,N
are related to the size of the hexagon. To study the large hexagon, one requires the sizes grow linearly in
n
as
n\to\infty
. Let

\frac{a}{n}\to t_{1},\quad,\frac{b}{n}\to t_{2},\quad\frac{N}{n}\to t_{3},\quad\text{ for some }t_{1},t_{2}>0.,t_{3}\geq 1.

The scaled Hahn weight is

\mu_{N}^{(a,b)}(x)=\binom{a+nx}{nx}\binom{b+N-nx}{N-nx},\quad,x=0,\frac{1}{n},\dots,\frac{N}{n}.

(427)

Then the scaled Hahn polynomials have the recurrence coefficients,

a_{j,n}=\frac{j(j+a+b+N+1)(j+b)}{N(2j+a+b)(2j+a+b+1)}\sqrt{\frac{(N-j)(j+a+b)(a+j)(2j+a+b+1)}{j(j+a+b+N+1)(b+j)(2j+a+b-1)}},

b_{j,n}=\frac{(N-j)(j+a+b+1)(j+a+1)}{N(2j+a+b+N+1)(2j+a+b+2)}.

Clearly, Condition
2.1
is satisfied for all recurrence coefficients with indices in the following set
I
,

I=\left\{j\in\mathbb{N}:\frac{j}{n}\to 1\right\}.

(428)

For both edges, clearly, the limits
\lim_{n}a_{n,n}\eqqcolon a_{0}
and
\lim_{n}b_{n-1,n}\eqqcolon b_{0}
exist, with the rate of convergence at most
O(n^{-1})
. Take
x_{0}=b_{0}\pm 2a_{0}
, apply Theorem
2.2
and we conclude that the asymptotics of the edge fluctuations holds for all
\alpha\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
.
